The disobedient sinner then turns away from blaming himself, repenting of his sins, and seeking refuge from the evil of his own soul. Indeed, he becomes inclined to invoke divine decree as an argument against Allah. That is an invalid argument which does him no good; rather, it only increases his torment and misery, just as it did for Satan when he said:

“Because You have led me astray, I will surely lie in wait for them on Your straight path.”

And he said:

“My Lord, because You have led me astray, I will surely make evil alluring to them on earth, and I will lead them all astray.”

Likewise are those who will say on the Day of Resurrection, “If only Allah had guided me, I would have been among

---

## Page 7328

the righteous.” Likewise are those who said:

“If Allah had willed, neither we nor our forefathers would have associated partners with Him, nor would we have forbidden anything.”

Whoever invokes divine decree to justify the sins he has committed, while turning away from what Allah has commanded—repentance, seeking forgiveness, seeking Allah’s help and refuge, and asking Him for guidance—is among the greatest losers in this world and the Hereafter. This, then, is one benefit of mentioning the distinction alongside what they have in common.

Section

The third distinction: Allah multiplies a good deed and causes it to grow, and rewards the intention to perform it. He does not multiply an evil deed, nor does He hold a person accountable for intending it. Thus, He grants the doer of good a reward exceeding what he has done, whereas He recompenses the doer of evil only in proportion to his deed. Allah Most High says:

“Whoever comes with a good deed will receive ten times its like, and whoever comes with an evil deed will be recompensed only with its like, and they will not be wronged.”

The fourth distinction: A good deed is ascribed to Him because He has bestowed it as a favor in every respect, as explained previously. There is no aspect of it that does not warrant its being ascribed to Him.

---

## Page 7329

As for an evil deed, He creates it only for a wise purpose, and in light of that purpose, it is an expression of His beneficence. For the Lord never does anything evil. Rather, His acts are all good and beneficent; all that He does is good. Thus, the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) would say in the opening supplication of prayer:

“All good is in Your hands, and evil is not ascribed to You.”

For He does not create pure evil. Rather, everything He creates serves a wise purpose, in light of which it is good. Yet it may involve evil for some people; this is partial and relative evil. As for universal or absolute evil, the Lord is far exalted above it. This is the evil that is not ascribed to Him.

As for partial and relative evil, it is good in light of the wise purpose it serves. Hence, evil is never ascribed to Him on its own. Rather, it is either included within the generality of created things, as in His words:

“And He created all things”;

or ascribed to its cause, as in His words:

“From the evil of what He has created”;

or its agent is left unmentioned, as in the words of the jinn:

“And we do not know whether evil is intended for those on earth, or whether their Lord intends right guidance for them.”

On this point, two groups of people who engage in false discourse concerning divine decree have gone astray:

---

## Page 7330

One group denied this and said: “He does not create people’s actions, nor does He will everything that occurs, because sins are reprehensible, and He does not do what is reprehensible. Willing them is reprehensible, and He does not will what is reprehensible.”

The other group recognized that He is the Creator of all this, yet did not believe that He created it for a wise purpose. Instead, they said: “If He creates this, then He may create every evil. He creates nothing for a wise purpose, and there is no act above which He is exalted. Rather, whatever is possible, He may do.”

They deemed it possible for Him to command every form of disbelief and disobedience, and to forbid all faith, obedience, truthfulness, and justice; to punish the prophets and grant bliss to the Pharaohs and the polytheists; and other such things. They made no distinction between one thing He creates and another. This is reprehensible speech and falsehood, just like the first view. Allah Most High says:

“Or do those who commit evil deeds think that We will make them like those who believe and do righteous deeds, equal in their life and their death? How evil is their judgment!”

And He Most High says:

“Shall We then treat the Muslims like the criminals?”

“What is the matter with you? How do you judge?”

And He Most High says:

“Or shall We treat those who believe and do righteous deeds like those who spread corruption on earth? Or shall We treat the righteous like the wicked?”

These and similar passages establish that He distinguishes between good deeds and evil deeds, and between the doer of good

---

## Page 7331

and the doer of evil, and that anyone who considers it possible for Him to treat them equally has made a reprehensible and false claim that must be rejected.

His creating something that harms some living creatures does not mean that it contains no wisdom. Rather, it contains wisdom and mercy hidden from some of them, whose full measure is known only to Allah. Nor does the occurrence within creation of something evil in a limited, relative sense make it an all-encompassing, universal evil. Rather, things that are universal and all-encompassing bring only good and benefit to His servants, such as widespread rain or the sending of a messenger to all.

This entails that Allah cannot support someone who lies about Him with the miracles by which He supported His truthful prophets. That would be an evil affecting everyone, misleading them and corrupting their religion, their worldly life, and their Hereafter. This is not like an unjust king or an enemy. For through an unjust king, Allah necessarily wards off evil greater than the injustice he inflicts.

It has been said: “Sixty years under an unjust ruler are better than a single night without a ruler.” And if

---

## Page 7332

his injustice is assumed to be extensive, that is a harm to religion, like calamities that expiate their sins and for which they are rewarded, and through which they turn back to Allah, seek His forgiveness, and repent to Him. The same applies to the enemies He gives power over them.

But as for someone who lies about Allah and says—that is, claims—that he is a prophet, were Allah to support him as He supports a truthful prophet, this would entail treating him and the truthful prophet equally. Guidance and misguidance, good and evil, and the path to Paradise and the path to Hell would then become indistinguishable. The distinction between the two would disappear. This would bring universal corruption upon people in their religion, their worldly life, and their Hereafter.

For this reason, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, commanded fighting those among the people of innovation who fight for a corrupt religion, such as the Khārijites. He commanded patience under the injustice of rulers and forbade fighting them or rebelling against them. Thus, Allah may grant many unjust kings power for a time.

As for lying claimants to prophethood, however, He does not prolong their hold on power. Rather, He will inevitably destroy them, because their corruption extends to religion, worldly life, and the Hereafter. Allah Most High said:

“Had he fabricated some sayings in Our name,”
“We would surely have seized him by the right hand,”
“then severed his aorta.”

And Allah Most High said: “Or do they say, ‘He has fabricated a lie about Allah’? If Allah willed, He could set a seal upon

---

## Page 7333

your heart.” He thus declared that, supposing such fabrication occurred, He would inevitably punish anyone who fabricated lies about Him.

Section

This is an issue concerning which people have fallen into confusion. The Qadarīs who deny divine decree and the proponents of compulsion argued that if it is possible for Him to lead one person astray, it is possible for Him to lead all people astray. If it is possible for Him to torment a living creature without its having sinned and without compensating it, it is possible for Him to torment every living being without its having sinned and without compensating it. And if it is possible for Him to withhold assistance in obeying His command from one person whom He has commanded, it is possible for Him to withhold assistance from all creation.

Thus, neither group distinguished between particular and universal evil, or between relative and absolute evil. Nor did they acknowledge any wise purpose in relative evil by virtue of which it would fall within the category of good.

The deniers then said: “It is known that He is exalted above such actions. For if we considered this possible for Him, we would also consider it possible for Him to support a liar with miracles, torment the prophets, honor the disbelievers, and do other things that rational people regard as too grievous to attribute to Allah Most High.”

---

## Page 7334

The Jahmīs who affirmed divine decree and upheld compulsion replied:

“Rather, all actions are possible for Him, just as that particular action is possible. It is only through reports—the prophets’ reports about Him—that we know He will not do what He does not do, or will do what He does. Otherwise, whatever action is supposed, He may either do it or refrain from doing it. In reality, there is no cause, wise purpose, or attribute that entails selecting some actions rather than others. There is only a will whose relation to all events is the same. One of two identical possibilities prevails without anything to favor it.”

They were then told:

“In that case, supporting a liar with a miracle would be possible. A miracle would therefore no longer be evidence of the prophets’ truthfulness, and no prophet’s report would remain by which the distinction could be known. Thus, besides disbelief in the prophets, your position entails that the distinction cannot be known through either revelation or reason.”

They therefore sought a way to distinguish miracles from other events, arguing that allowing the possibility of a liar producing miracles would entail declaring the Creator Most High incapable of providing a means of distinguishing the truthful from the liar; or that miracles are self-evidently known to indicate truthfulness.

This matter has been discussed at length elsewhere. The error of both groups has been explained, as has the fact that those who followed Jahm in upholding compulsion—and denied Allah’s wisdom and mercy, the causes through which He acts, and the powers and other things He has created—are innovators who oppose the Book, the Sunnah, and the consensus of the early generations, while also contradicting the plain dictates of reason.

Likewise, the Qadarīs who deny divine decree oppose the Book, the Sunnah, and the consensus of the early generations, while also contradicting the plain dictates of reason.

---

## Page 7335

Section

The purpose here is to discuss His words:

“Whatever good befalls you is from Allah, and whatever evil befalls you is from yourself.”

This entails that the servant should continually give thanks and seek forgiveness.

It has already been mentioned that evil is attributed to Allah only in one of three ways. Al-Fātihah encompasses all three categories. He—glory be to Him—is the Most Merciful, whose mercy encompasses all things. An authentic hadith from the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) states:

“He is more merciful to His servants than a mother is to her child.”

His mercy precedes and prevails over His wrath. He is the All-Forgiving, the Most Loving, the Forbearing, the Merciful.

His will is the source of every good and blessing, and every good and blessing comes from Him:

“Whatever blessing you have is from Allah.”

He—glory be to Him—has said:

“Tell My servants that I am indeed the All-Forgiving, the Most Merciful.”

Then He said:

“And that My punishment is indeed the painful punishment.”

And He Most High said:

“Know that Allah is severe in punishment, and that Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”

Forgiveness and mercy are thus among His attributes mentioned

---

## Page 7336

in His names. They are therefore necessitated by His holy Essence, entailed by it and inseparable from it.

As for punishment, it is among His creations. He created it for a wise purpose; considered in relation to that purpose, it is wisdom and mercy. Thus, no good comes to a person except from his Lord, through His kindness and generosity, and no evil comes to him except from himself. Whatever good befalls him is from Allah, and whatever evil befalls him is from himself.

In His words, “And whatever befalls you,” the second-person pronoun may address the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace), as Ibn ʿAbbās and others said. This is the more apparent interpretation, because He says afterwards:

“And We have sent you to mankind as a Messenger.”

Alternatively, it may address each individual human being, as in His words:

“O mankind, what has deceived you concerning your Most Generous Lord?”

But this interpretation is weak, for there has been no preceding mention of mankind here, nor any occasion for mentioning mankind. Rather, what preceded was a reference to a group who said what they said. Had the intention been to refer to them, it would have said: “Whatever good befalls them is from Allah, and whatever evil befalls them.” Instead, the Messenger was addressed in these words because he is the foremost of Adam’s descendants. If this applies to him, it applies all the more emphatically to others. This is like His words:

“Fear Allah, and do not obey the disbelievers and the hypocrites,”

and His words, Most High:

“If you associate partners with Allah

---

## Page 7337

your deeds will surely come to nothing,” and His words:

“So if you are in doubt about what We have revealed to you, ask those who read the Scripture before you.”

Such address is of two types. In one, the wording applies specifically to him, but extends to others all the more emphatically, as in His words:

“O Prophet, why do you forbid what Allah has made lawful for you, seeking to please your wives?”

Then He says:

“Allah has ordained for you a way to release yourselves from your oaths.”

In the other type, an address to him may constitute an address to all people. As many Quran commentators say, “The address is to him, but others are intended.” This does not mean that he himself was not addressed. Rather, he is foremost among those addressed. Thus, addressing him constitutes an address to the entire human race, even though he himself does not commit what he has been forbidden or neglect what he has been commanded. Rather, it is others who do so.

This is like a ruler saying to a commander, “Set out tomorrow for such-and-such a place,” meaning: you and the troops accompanying you. Likewise, when he forbids something to the person he holds in highest esteem, this constitutes a prohibition for those below him. This is a familiar manner of address.

Thus, in His words:

“Whatever good befalls you is from Allah, and whatever evil befalls you is from yourself,”

the address is to him (may Allah bless him and grant him peace), and all creation is included in

---

## Page 7338

this address through its general application, and all the more emphatically. This differs from His words:

“And We have sent you as a Messenger to mankind.”

This applies exclusively to him. Nevertheless, whoever conveys his message is included in the meaning of the address. As he (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) said:

“Convey from me, even if only one verse.”

He also said:

“May Allah brighten the face of a person who hears a hadith from us and conveys it to someone who has not heard it.”

And he said:

“Let those present convey it to those absent.”

And:

“The scholars are the heirs of the prophets.”

Allah Most High has also said in the Quran:

“This Quran has been revealed to me so that I may warn you through it, and whomever it reaches.”

The point here is that “good” is attributed to Him, glorified is He, in every respect. “Evil” is attributed to Him because He created it, just as He created “good.” Hence He said:

“All is from Allah.”

Yet He created it only for a wise purpose. It is not attributed to Him insofar as it is evil. Rather, it is attributed to the soul, which does evil through it, not for a wise purpose. It therefore deserves to have evil and bad things attributed to it. For in committing sins, the soul does not seek a good that would make committing them preferable for its sake. Rather, anything of that kind belongs among good deeds. Thus Allah’s actions are good: He never does anything foul or evil.

This encompasses both evils suffered as recompense and evil deeds. For His words, “Whatever good befalls you” and “whatever evil,” refer to blessings and calamities, as explained earlier. But if a calamity comes from a person’s own self because he has sinned, then the sin itself comes from his own self all the more clearly. Thus evils undoubtedly come from his own self.

It is only alongside good that He attributes them to Himself, in His words:

---

## Page 7339

“All is from Allah,” as explained earlier. For evils are not attributed to Allah on their own, but only in general terms, as in His words:

“All is from Allah.”

Likewise, names that mention evil are used only in pairs, as when we say, “the Bringer of Harm and the Giver of Benefit, the Giver and the Withholder, the Bestower of Honor and the Humiliator,” or with a qualification, as in His words:

“Indeed, We shall exact retribution from the criminals.”

Everything He has created that contains some limited, relative evil also contains general good, wisdom, and mercy many times greater than that evil. An example is His sending Moses to Pharaoh. This resulted in rejection and destruction for Pharaoh and his people, which was evil in relation to them. Yet it also brought widespread benefit to creation until the Day of Resurrection, through the lessons drawn from Pharaoh’s story. This constitutes a general good, and those who benefited from it outnumbered those harmed by it many times over. As Allah Most High said:

“When they provoked Our anger, We exacted retribution from them and drowned them all. We made them a precedent and an example for later generations.”

After recounting his story, Allah Most High also said:

“Indeed, in that is a lesson for whoever fears Allah.”

Likewise, the message of Muhammad (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) brought misery upon a group of Arab polytheists and disbelievers among the People of the Book: those who rejected him and whom Allah Most High destroyed on his account. Yet those who attained happiness through his message outnumbered them many times over. For this reason, those among the People of the Book who suffered misery on his account had already been making alterations and distortions before

---

## Page 7340

Allah sent Muhammad (may Allah bless him and grant him peace). Allah destroyed a group through jihad, while many times their number among the People of the Book were guided through him. As for those among the People of the Book whom Allah humbled through subjugation and humiliation, or the polytheists upon whom He imposed humiliation, their subjugation was a mercy to them, lest their disbelief grow greater and their evil increase. Then, after them, others received guidance and mercy in numbers known only to Allah. People from among them continue to be guided, one group after another, through the blessing of his religion’s ascendancy by proof and force. The benefit of sending him, granting him might, and making his religion prevail brought mercy incomparably greater than the limited, relative evil that this brought upon some people, especially given the goodness and wisdom contained even in that. For nothing Allah, glorified be He, has created is pure evil; rather, its evil is relative.

Section

The Fifth Distinction

All the good deeds a person performs are positive realities with which Allah has blessed him. They come about through Allah’s will, mercy, wisdom, power, and creative act. There is nothing in good deeds that is merely an absence and is not attributable to

---

## Page 7341

Allah. Rather, all of them are positive realities. Everything that exists or comes into being is brought into being by Allah.

This is because good deeds consist either in performing what is commanded or in refraining from what is forbidden. Refraining is itself a positive reality. A person’s refraining from what he has been forbidden, his knowledge that it is an evil sin and a cause of punishment, his hatred and aversion toward it, and his restraining himself from it when his soul desires, craves, and seeks it—all these are positive realities. Likewise, his knowledge that good deeds—such as justice and truthfulness—are good, and his performance of them, are positive realities.

Thus, a person is rewarded for performing good deeds only when he does them out of love for them, deliberately intending thereby to seek the Face of his Lord and to obey Allah and His Messenger. He is rewarded for refraining from evil deeds when he abandons them out of aversion to them and restrains himself from them. Allah Most High says:

“But Allah has endeared faith to you and made it beautiful in your hearts, and has made disbelief, wickedness, and disobedience hateful to you. Those are the rightly guided.”

Allah Most High also says:

“But as for him who feared standing before his Lord and restrained his soul from desire,”

“Paradise will indeed be his refuge.”

And Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, prayer restrains one from indecency and wrongdoing.”

In the two Sahīh collections, Anas reports that the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) said: “Whoever possesses three qualities will taste the sweetness of faith: that Allah and His Messenger are dearer to him than all else; that he loves a person only for Allah’s sake; and that he would hate

---

## Page 7342

to return to disbelief—after Allah has rescued him from it—as much as he would hate to be thrown into the Fire.”

In the Sunan collections, al-Barā’ ibn ‘Āzib reports that the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) said:

“The firmest bonds of faith are loving for Allah’s sake and hating for Allah’s sake.”

They also contain a report from Abū Umāmah that the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) said:

“Whoever loves for Allah’s sake, hates for Allah’s sake, gives for Allah’s sake, and withholds for Allah’s sake has perfected his faith.”

In the Sahīh, Abū Sa‘īd al-Khudrī reports that the Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) said:

“Whoever among you sees a wrong, let him change it with his hand. If he cannot, then with his tongue. If he cannot, then with his heart. That is the weakest level of faith.”

Also in the Sahīh, in the hadith of Ibn Mas‘ūd (may Allah be pleased with him), the Prophet said, after mentioning the successors:

“Whoever strives against them with his hand is a believer. Whoever strives against them with his tongue is a believer. Whoever strives against them with his heart is a believer. Beyond that there is not even a mustard seed’s weight of faith.”

Allah Most High says:

“You have had an excellent example in Abraham and those with him, when they said to their people, ‘We disavow you and whatever you worship besides Allah. We reject you, and enmity and hatred have arisen between us and you forever, until you believe in Allah alone’—except for Abraham’s saying to his father, ‘I will surely ask forgiveness for you, but I can do nothing to protect you from Allah.’”

---

## Page 7343

And He said through His close friend Abraham:

“Indeed, I disavow whatever you worship, except Him who created me; He will surely guide me.”

And He said:

“Have you considered what you have been worshipping—you and your forefathers? They are enemies to me, except the Lord of all worlds.”

And He said:

“When it set, he said, ‘O my people, I disavow whatever you associate with Allah. Indeed, I have turned my face toward Him who created the heavens and the earth, inclining to true faith, and I am not one of the polytheists.’”

This hatred, enmity, and disavowal of whatever is worshipped besides Allah and of its worshippers are present in the heart and expressed through the tongue and limbs. Likewise, love for Allah, loyalty to Him, and loyalty to His allies are present in the heart and expressed through the tongue and limbs. These give effect to the declaration, “There is no god but Allah.” This means affirming the heart’s worship of Allah with pure love and genuine submission, preventing it from worshipping anything besides Allah, and hating and detesting such worship.

Thus, a person worships Allah alone, loves to worship Him, and hates the worship of anything else. He loves to place his trust in Him, fear Him, and call upon Him, and hates placing his trust in anyone else, fearing them, or calling upon them. All these are things present in the heart. They are the good deeds for which Allah grants reward.

As for merely not committing evil deeds, without knowing that they are evil or disliking them, but simply not doing them because they have never crossed one’s mind, or they cross one’s mind just as

---

## Page 7344

inanimate objects that he neither loves nor hates do—such a person receives no reward for not committing evil deeds. Yet neither is he punished for committing them; it is as though he had not committed them. In his case, evil deeds are thus treated as they are in the case of a child, an insane person, or an animal: there is neither reward nor punishment. However, once the proof has been established against him through his knowledge that these deeds are forbidden, unless he believes in their prohibition and abhors them, he is punished for failing to believe that they are forbidden.

Section

People have disagreed about refraining from action: is it something positively existent, or merely an absence? Most hold that it is something positively existent. A group, however—including Abū Hāshim ibn al-Jubbā’ī—maintains that it is merely an absence, and that someone who has been commanded to act is punished simply for not performing the action, rather than for an act of refraining that occurs within him. They are called “al-Madhammiyya” because they attach blame to sheer absence.

The majority say that refraining from action is something positively existent. Thus, no reward is given to one who

---

## Page 7345

refrains from what is forbidden, except for an act of refraining that occurs within him.

Likewise, one who fails to perform what is commanded is punished only for an act of refraining that occurs within him: the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, commands him to act, but he refuses. This refusal is something positively existent. Thus, he occupies himself with the opposite of what he was commanded to do, just as he occupies himself with worshipping others instead of worshipping Allah alone. He is punished for that.

Hence, everyone who does not worship Allah alone must necessarily worship someone else. By worshipping another, he associates partners with Allah. There is no third category among the children of Adam: a person either worships Allah alone, associates partners with Him, or mixes the two, as do those among the religious communities who have altered their faith—the Christians and the misguided people professing Islam who resemble them. Allah Most High says:

“So when you recite the Quran, seek refuge in Allah from Satan, the accursed. Indeed, he has no authority over those who believe and put their trust in their Lord. His authority is only over those who take him as an ally and those who, through him, associate others with Allah.”

The Most High also says:

“Indeed, you have no authority over My servants, except those of the wayward who follow you.”

When Iblīs said:

“I will surely make things on earth attractive to them, and I will surely lead them all astray, except Your chosen servants among them,”

the Most High said:

“Indeed, you have no authority over My servants, except those of the wayward who follow you.”

Thus, Iblīs does not lead Allah’s chosen servants astray, nor does he have authority over them. His authority is only over the wayward: those who take him as an ally and who, through him, associate others with Allah.

---

## Page 7346

His words, “those who take him as an ally and those who, through him, associate others with Allah,” describe one and the same group. Everyone who takes him as an ally associates others with Allah through him, and everyone who associates others with Allah through him has taken him as an ally. Allah Most High says:

“Did I not enjoin upon you, O children of Adam, that you should not worship Satan—for he is your clear enemy—and that you should worship Me? This is a straight path.”

Everyone who worships anything other than Allah is in fact worshipping Satan, even if he thinks he is worshipping angels or prophets. Allah Most High says:

“On the Day He gathers them all together, He will say to the angels, ‘Was it you that these people used to worship?’ They will say, ‘Glory be to You! You are our Protector, not they. Rather, they used to worship the jinn; most of them believed in them.’”

This is why devils appear to those who worship angels, prophets and the righteous, and speak to them. They then imagine that the one who addressed them was an angel, a prophet or a friend of Allah, when he is merely a devil posing as an angel. This also happens to star-worshippers and practitioners of incantations and talismans. They invoke names which they claim are the names of angels, such as Mantatrūn and others, whereas they are only the names of jinn.

Likewise, those who invoke created beings—prophets, friends of Allah and angels—may have someone appear to them and address them. They imagine him to be the prophet or righteous person whom they invoked. Yet in reality

---

## Page 7347

he is a devil who has assumed that person’s appearance, or who has said, “I am he,” to someone who does not know what the person he invoked looks like. This frequently happens to those who invoke created beings, whether Christians or those who profess Islam: they invoke them at their graves or in their absence and seek their aid.

Someone then appears to them in human form, either riding or on foot, claiming to be the one whose aid they sought. The person seeking aid believes that this is that prophet or righteous person, or his inner secret, his spiritual presence, or his raqīqa, or that a meaning has taken form. Alternatively, he says that it is an angel who has appeared in that person’s likeness. Yet it is only a devil leading him astray, because he has associated others with Allah and invoked someone other than Him—the dead or those beneath them. Through that association, Satan has gained authority over him. He imagines that he is invoking the prophet, righteous person or angel, and that this is the one who interceded for him or answered his supplication. In reality, it is only Satan, seeking to drive him further into disbelief and misguidance.

Thus, whoever does not worship Allah with sincere devotion to Him alone must be associating others with Him and worshipping something other than Allah. In reality, he is worshipping Satan. Every descendant of Adam is therefore either a worshipper of the Most Merciful or a worshipper of Satan. Allah Most High says:

“Whoever turns away from the remembrance of the Most Merciful, We appoint for him a devil who becomes his companion.”

“Indeed, they turn them away from the path, while they think they are rightly guided.”

“Until, when he comes to Us, he says, ‘If only there were between me and you the distance between east and west! What an evil companion!’”

“And never

---

## Page 7348

will your sharing in the punishment benefit you today, since you did wrong.”

Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, those who believe, the Jews, the Sabians, the Christians, the Magians, and those who associate others with Allah—Allah will judge between them on the Day of Resurrection. Indeed, Allah is Witness over all things.”

Thus, all descendants of Adam fall within these six categories. A fuller discussion of this belongs elsewhere.

Section

The point here is that reward and punishment relate only to actual deeds: performing good deeds, such as worshipping Allah alone; refraining from evil deeds, such as refraining from associating partners with Him, which is itself an actual act; and committing evil deeds, such as abandoning monotheism and worshipping others besides Allah, which is also an actual act.

Allah Most High says:

“Whoever comes with a good deed will have better than it; but whoever comes with an evil deed—those who commit evil deeds will be recompensed only for what they used to do.”

And He, Most High, says:

“If you do good, you do good for yourselves; and if you do evil, it is against yourselves.”

And He, Most High, says:

“Whoever does righteous deeds, it is for his own benefit; and whoever does evil, it is to his own detriment.”

And He, Most High, says:

“For those who do good is the best reward—and more. Neither darkness nor humiliation will cover their faces. Those are the inhabitants of Paradise; they will abide therein forever.”

“But those who earn evil deeds—the recompense of an evil deed is its like, and humiliation will cover them”—up to His words—“Those are the inhabitants of the Fire; they will abide therein forever.”

And He, Most High, says:

---

## Page 7349

“Then the end of those who did evil was the worst, because they denied Allah’s signs and used to ridicule them.”

As for the absence of both good and evil deeds, its recompense is the absence of both reward and punishment. Suppose a man believes in the Messenger in general terms and, for a time, does not commit many prohibited acts, yet has never heard that they are prohibited and therefore does not believe them to be so. For example, someone may believe without knowing that Allah has forbidden carrion, blood, and pork, or that He has forbidden marriage to relatives except for four categories, or that He has forbidden four categories through marriage ties—prohibiting each spouse from marrying the other’s ascendants and descendants. If he believes and does not commit these prohibited acts, yet does not believe them to be prohibited because he has never heard this, he receives neither reward nor punishment for this.

But if he learns of their prohibition and accepts it as such, he is rewarded for that belief. If he also refrains from them despite his own desire for them, he receives an additional reward. This is like someone whose soul urges him to pursue desires, but he restrains it: a fasting person who desires food and sexual intercourse but restrains himself, or someone who desires wine and indecent acts but restrains himself. Such a person receives an additional reward according to the extent to which he restrains himself, patiently resists forbidden things, and occupies himself with acts of obedience that are their opposites. When he performs those acts of obedience, they prevent him from committing forbidden acts.

---

## Page 7350

Once this is clear, all the good deeds for which a person is rewarded have a positive existence, as a blessing from Allah Most High. Whatever good the soul loves and whatever evil it hates, it is He who has endeared faith to the believers and adorned it in their hearts, and made disbelief, wickedness, and disobedience hateful to them.

Section

As for evil deeds, they originate in ignorance and wrongdoing. No one commits an evil, reprehensible deed except because he does not know that it is evil and reprehensible, or because of his desire and his soul’s inclination toward it. Nor does anyone neglect an obligatory good deed except because he does not know that it is obligatory, or because his soul dislikes it.

In reality, all evil deeds ultimately stem from ignorance. Otherwise, if a person possessed beneficial knowledge that doing something would cause him harm outweighing its benefit, he would not do it. For this is characteristic of a rational person. Thus, if there is among good deeds something he knows would cause him harm outweighing its benefit—such as falling from a high place or into a river that would drown him, passing beside a leaning wall, entering a blazing fire, throwing his wealth into the sea, and the like—

---

## Page 7351

he would not do it, knowing that it entails harm without benefit. Someone who does not know that it will harm him—such as a child, an insane person, or someone who is distracted or heedless—may do so. Whoever undertakes something harmful, despite knowing the harm it entails, does so because he believes its benefit outweighs its harm. Either he is certain of a harm outweighed by benefit, or he believes that the good predominates. Thus, the good must predominate, either in probability or in the anticipated outcome. This is like someone who travels by sea and undertakes long journeys for profit: if he were certain that he would drown or suffer a loss, he would not travel. Rather, he considers safety and profit more likely, even if this belief is mistaken.

The same applies to sins. If a thief were certain that he would be caught and have his hand cut off, he would not steal. Likewise, if someone committing unlawful sexual intercourse were certain that he would be stoned to death, he would not commit it. The case of someone who drinks intoxicants differs: he may risk forty or eighty lashes and nevertheless persist in drinking.

For this reason, the correct view is that the punishment for drinking intoxicants is not fixed; rather, it may extend to execution if he will not desist otherwise, as stated in the hadiths and discussed elsewhere.

The same applies to punishments generally: whenever someone intent on sinning is certain that, through it, he will incur

---

## Page 7352

harm outweighing any benefit, he will not commit it. Rather, either he is not certain that it is forbidden, or he is not certain that he will be punished for it. Instead, he hopes to be forgiven on account of good deeds or repentance, or through Allah’s pardon. Or he is heedless of all this, calling to mind neither the prohibition nor the threatened punishment. Thus he remains heedless, without the prohibition in mind. Heedlessness is one of the opposites of knowledge.

Section

Heedlessness and desire are the root of evil. Allah Most High says:

“And do not obey one whose heart We have made heedless of Our remembrance, who follows his own desires and whose conduct exceeds all bounds.”

Desire alone does not suffice to bring about evil deeds unless accompanied by ignorance. Otherwise, when someone driven by desire knows with certainty that something will cause him harm outweighing any benefit, his soul naturally turns away from it. For Allah Most High has instilled in the soul a love for what benefits it and a hatred for what harms it. It therefore does not do what it knows with certainty will cause it harm outweighing any benefit. Rather, whenever it does so, this is due to weakness of reason. This is why such a person is described as rational, discerning, and possessed of sound judgment. This is also why the great affliction comes from Satan, not merely from the soul itself. For

---

## Page 7353

Satan makes evil deeds seem appealing to the soul, urges it to commit them, and points out their attractive features—those that bring benefit rather than harm—just as Iblīs did with Adam and Eve, saying:

“O Adam, shall I direct you to the tree of immortality and a kingdom that never decays?”

“So they both ate from it, and their nakedness became apparent to them.”

“He said, ‘Your Lord only forbade you this tree to prevent you from becoming angels or becoming immortal.’”

For this reason, Allah Most High says:

“Whoever turns away from the remembrance of the Most Merciful, We appoint for him a devil who becomes his companion.”

“Indeed, they turn them away from the path, while they think they are rightly guided.”

Allah Most High also says:

“What of one whose evil deeds have been made appealing to him, so that he regards them as good?”

And Allah Most High says:

“Do not insult those they invoke besides Allah, lest they insult Allah in hostility, without knowledge. Thus We have made every community’s deeds appealing to them. Then to their Lord they will return, and He will inform them of what they used to do.”

His words, “We have made every community’s deeds appealing to them,” refer to this occurring through the angels, prophets, and believers making good seem appealing, and through the devils among jinn and mankind making evil seem appealing. Allah Most High says:

“Likewise, their partners have made the killing of their children seem appealing to many of the polytheists, to bring them to ruin and leave them confused about their religion.”

Thus, what fundamentally leads people into evil deeds is ignorance: not knowing that these deeds bring them harm outweighing any benefit, or believing that they bring them benefit outweighing any harm. This is why it was said by

---

## Page 7354

the Companions, may Allah be pleased with them:

“Everyone who disobeys Allah is ignorant.”

This is how they explained His words, Most High:

“Allah only accepts the repentance of those who do evil in ignorance and then repent soon afterwards.”

Likewise, He says:

“When those who believe in Our revelations come to you, say, ‘Peace be upon you. Your Lord has prescribed mercy for Himself: whoever among you does evil in ignorance, then repents afterwards and mends his ways—He is indeed All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.’”

For this reason, the state of committing evil deeds is called jāhiliyyah (ignorance), for it is accompanied by a condition characteristic of jāhiliyyah.

Abū al-ʿĀliyah said:

“I asked the Companions of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, about this verse: ‘Allah only accepts the repentance of those who do evil in ignorance and then repent soon afterwards.’ They said: ‘Everyone who disobeys Allah is ignorant, and whoever repents shortly before death has indeed repented soon afterwards.’”

Qatādah said:

“The Companions of Muhammad, the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, unanimously agreed that everyone who disobeys his Lord does so in ignorance, whether deliberately or not. Everyone who disobeys Allah is ignorant.”

The Successors and those after them said the same.

Mujāhid said:

“Anyone who commits a sin, whether old or young, does so in ignorance.”

He also said:

“Whoever disobeys his Lord is ignorant until he abandons his disobedience.”

He further said:

“It means applying the term ‘ignorance’ to deliberate action.”

Mujāhid also said:

“Whoever does evil mistakenly or commits a sin deliberately is ignorant until he abandons it.”

These reports were narrated by Ibn

---

## Page 7355

Abī Hātim.

He then said:

Similar statements were also reported from Qatādah, ‘Amr ibn Murrah, and al-Thawrī: “Whether mistakenly or deliberately.”

It was reported that Mujāhid and al-Dahhāk said:

“His ignorance does not consist in his not knowing what is lawful or unlawful; rather, his ignorance lies in his committing the sin.”

‘Ikrimah said:

“The whole of worldly life is ignorance.”

Al-Hasan al-Basrī was asked about it and said:

“They are people who did not know what was in their favor and what counted against them.”

He was asked, “What if they did know?”

He replied, “Then let them abandon it, for it is ignorance.”

I say: Among the things that clarify this are the words of Allah Most High:

“Only those of His servants who have knowledge fear Allah.”

Everyone who fears Him, obeys Him, and refrains from disobeying Him is knowledgeable. As Allah Most High says:

“Is one who is devoutly obedient during the hours of the night, prostrating and standing in prayer, fearing the Hereafter and hoping for his Lord’s mercy [like one who is not]? Say, ‘Are those who know equal to those who do not know?’”

A man addressed al-Sha‘bī, saying, “O scholar!”

He replied, “The truly knowledgeable person is only one who fears Allah.”

The words of Allah Most High, “Only those of His servants who have knowledge fear Allah,” imply that everyone who fears Allah is knowledgeable, for none but a knowledgeable person fears Him.

---

## Page 7356

And it also implies that the knowledgeable person is one who fears Allah, as the early generations said. Ibn Mas‘ūd said:

“Fear of Allah is sufficient as knowledge, and delusion is sufficient as ignorance.”

Such exclusivity can operate in both directions: restricting the first to the second—which holds universally—and restricting the second to the first. Examples include His words:

“You can only warn those who follow the Reminder and fear the Most Merciful unseen.”

“You are only a warner to those who fear it.”

“Only those believe in Our signs who, when reminded of them, fall down in prostration and glorify their Lord with praise, and are not arrogant.”

“Their sides forsake their beds.”

This is because He affirmed fear of Allah for the knowledgeable and denied it of others. This resembles an exception: according to the majority of scholars, an exception to a negative statement constitutes an affirmation. Examples include our saying, “None has the right to be worshipped but Allah,” and His words, Most High:

“They intercede only for those with whom He is pleased.”

“Intercession with Him benefits none except those for whom He gives permission.”

“They bring you no argument without Our bringing you the truth and the best explanation.”

Some scholars, however, hold that the excepted case is left unaddressed: what is stated is neither affirmed nor denied of it. They maintain that this applies all the more to formulations of exclusivity. Thus they say:

“He denied fear of Allah to those other than the knowledgeable, but did not affirm it for the knowledgeable.”

---

## Page 7357

But the correct view is that of the majority. This is like His saying:

“Say, ‘My Lord has only forbidden indecencies, whether open or concealed, sin, and unjust aggression.’”

This denies that anything outside these categories is forbidden and affirms that they are forbidden. But does He affirm fear of Allah for the knowledgeable as a class, or for every individual scholar? Compare the statements: “Only Muslims perform Hajj” and “No one performs Hajj except a Muslim.” The question is whether the excepted case entails the predicate or is a condition for it.

In this verse and others like it, it entails the predicate, so the statement applies universally. For knowledge of what the messengers warned against necessarily produces fear. Since knowledge produces the fear that prompts one to do good deeds and refrain from evil deeds, and every sinner is ignorant and lacks complete knowledge, this demonstrates what we have stated: the root of evil deeds is ignorance and lack of knowledge. That being so, lack of knowledge is not something that exists. Rather, it is like the absence of ability, hearing, sight, and all other privations.

Nonexistence has no agent and is not itself a thing; a thing is only that which exists. Allah Most High is the Creator of all things. Pure nonexistence, therefore, cannot be attributed to Allah. Yet something that exists may accompany it. If a person has no knowledge of Allah, he is not prompted by it to do good deeds and refrain from evil deeds. The soul is by nature continually changing, for it is alive, and will and voluntary movement are among

---

## Page 7358

the necessary attributes of life. Hence the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) said in an authentic hadith:

“The truest names are Hārith and Hammām.”

Every human being is a Hārith—that is, someone who works and earns—and a Hammām—that is, someone who forms intentions and wills. Thus, he moves by his own will. It is also stated in a hadith:

“The heart is like a feather lying in an open wilderness, and the heart churns more vigorously than a pot at a rolling boil.”

Since will and action are inherent in the soul, when Allah guides it, He teaches it what benefits it and what harms it. It then desires what benefits it and abandons what harms it.

Section

Allah, glorified is He, has bestowed upon the children of Adam two things that form the foundation of happiness.

The first is that every child is born with the natural disposition, as reported in the two Sahīh collections from the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him), who said:

“Every child is born with the natural disposition. Then his parents make him a Jew, a Christian, or a Zoroastrian, just as an animal gives birth to offspring with its body intact. Do you see any mutilation in it?”

Abū Hurayrah would then say: “Recite, if you wish: ‘The natural disposition Allah has instilled in mankind.’”

Allah Most High says:

“So turn your face toward the religion in pure devotion to Allah—the natural disposition Allah has instilled in mankind. There is no changing Allah’s creation. That is the upright religion.”

---

## Page 7359

And in Sahīh Muslim, ʿIyād ibn Himār reports that the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) said:

“Allah Most High says: ‘I created My servants with pure devotion to Me, but the devils led them astray. They forbade them what I had made lawful for them and commanded them to associate with Me that for which I had sent down no authority.’”

Thus, if left to its natural disposition, the soul acknowledges Allah as God, loves Him, and worships Him without associating anything with Him. Yet it is corrupted by the falsehood that the devils among humans and jinn make alluring to it, inspiring one another with it. Allah Most High says:

“And when your Lord took from the children of Adam—from their loins—their descendants and made them testify concerning themselves, ‘Am I not your Lord?’ They said, ‘Yes, we testify.’ This was so that you could not say on the Day of Resurrection, ‘We were unaware of this,’ or say, ‘It was only our forefathers who associated others with Allah, and we were their descendants after them. Will You then destroy us for what those who followed falsehood did?’”

The explanation of this verse is set out at length elsewhere.

The second:

Allah Most High has granted people general guidance through the knowledge and means of learning He has placed within their natural disposition, and through the Books He has sent down to them and the messengers He has sent to them. Allah Most High says:

“Read in the name of your Lord who created—
created man from a clinging clot.
Read! And your Lord is the Most Generous,
who taught by the pen,
taught man what he did not know.”

And Allah Most High says:

“The Most Merciful
taught the Quran,
created man,
taught him

---

## Page 7360

speech.”

And Allah Most High says:

“Glorify the name of your Lord, the Most High,
who created and proportioned,
who determined and guided.”

And Allah Most High says:

“And We guided him to the two paths.”

Thus, within everyone there is something that calls for knowledge and love of the truth. His Lord has guided him to various kinds of knowledge through which he can attain happiness in this world and the Hereafter, and has placed a love for this within his natural disposition. Yet, through his ignorance and heedlessness, a person may turn away from seeking knowledge of what benefits him.

His failure to seek or desire this is a mere absence and is not attributed to Allah Most High. Thus, neither his lack of knowledge of the truth nor his lack of desire for good is attributed to Allah.

As explained earlier, however, will and movement are inseparable from the soul, for it possesses natural life. Yet its happiness and salvation are attained only by living a beneficial and complete life. Its natural life becomes a cause of its punishment: it is neither alive, enjoying life, nor dead, relieved of punishment. Allah Most High says:

“So remind, if the reminder benefits.
He who fears will heed the reminder,
but the most wretched will shun it—
he who will burn in the greatest Fire,
where he will neither die nor live.”

Recompense, then, is of the same nature as the deed. Since, in this world, he did not live the beneficial life for which he was created,

---

## Page 7361

but rather lived as beasts do, and was not dead and devoid of sensation, his state in the Hereafter will be the same. For the purpose of life is to attain what benefits the living being and brings it pleasure. A living being inevitably experiences either pleasure or pain. If it experiences no pleasure, the purpose of life remains unfulfilled, since pain is not what is sought. This is like someone who is alive in this world but suffers from severe illnesses that prevent him from enjoying anything that the living enjoy. Throughout his life, he continues to prefer death, yet cannot attain it.

Since willing and acting are inseparable from the soul’s nature—for a human being is ever acting and intending—if it knows the True One, seeks Him, loves Him, and worships Him, this is part of Allah’s perfect bestowal of blessings upon it. Otherwise, its nature inevitably requires it to have an object of desire and worship other than Allah, and evil desires that harm it.

This evil thus arises from two things: its failure to know Allah and worship Him—which is an absence and is not attributed to an agent—and its inherent need for an object of desire and worship, which leads it to worship another besides Him. This is the evil for which it is punished. It results from its nature together with its lack of guidance.

The Qadariyyah acknowledge all of this, and acknowledge that Allah created the human being with a will. However, they hold that what is created is merely his potential and capacity to will—that is, his capacity to will this or that.

---

## Page 7362

As for his willing this or that particular thing, they hold that this is not created by Allah. In this they have gravely erred, for Allah is the Creator of all of this. The soul’s willing the sins it desires and its committing them are among the things Allah Most High has created, for Allah is the Creator of everything. It is He who inspired the soul—which He fashioned—with its wickedness and its righteousness.

{The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, would say in his supplication: “O Allah, grant my soul its righteousness and purify it; You are the best to purify it. You are its Guardian and Master.”}

He, glorified be He, made Abraham and his family leaders who guided by His command. He also made Pharaoh and his people leaders who called to the Fire, and on the Day of Resurrection they will not be helped.

Yet this is not attributed to Allah Most High in isolation, for two reasons: one concerns its final cause—its purpose—and the other its originating cause and efficient cause.

As for its purpose, Allah created it only for a wise purpose, in light of which it is good, not evil, even though it may be evil in a relative sense. If it is attributed to Him in isolation, someone might mistakenly understand this according to Jahm’s doctrine: that Allah creates pure evil containing no good for anyone, with neither wisdom nor mercy behind it. The transmitted reports, the Sunnah, and reasoned reflection refute this doctrine.

---

## Page 7363

Likewise, if someone says, “Muhammad and his community shed blood and spread corruption on earth,” this would be a condemnation of them, and it would be false. But if someone says, “They fight in Allah’s cause so that Allah’s word may be supreme and all worship may be devoted to Allah alone, and they kill those who prevent them from doing so,” this would be praise of them, and it would be true.

Thus, if someone says, “The Lord, blessed and exalted is He, is Wise and Merciful. He has made everything He created excellent and perfected all that He has fashioned. He is the Most Merciful of those who show mercy, more merciful to His servants than a mother is to her child. All good is in His hands, and evil is not ascribed to Him. Rather, He does nothing but good. Whatever pain He has created for some living beings, or whatever blameworthy deeds of theirs He has created, He has great wisdom and immense blessings in these things”—this would be true, and it would constitute praise and commendation of the Lord.

But if someone says, “He creates evil in which there is no good or benefit for anyone, with neither wisdom nor mercy, and He punishes people without their having sinned,” this would not constitute praise or commendation of the Lord, but the very opposite.

Some of these people even say, “Allah Most High is more harmful to His creation than Iblīs.” A detailed explanation of the falsity of their claims belongs elsewhere.

Regarding the creation of Hell, Iblīs, and evil deeds, we have already explained some of the wisdom

---

## Page 7364

and mercy. What we do not know is greater than what we know. So blessed is Allah, the best of creators, the Most Merciful of those who show mercy, the best of those who forgive, and Master of the Day of Judgment; the One, the Self-Sufficient upon whom all depend, who neither begets nor is begotten, and to whom none is equal. His servants cannot fully recount His praises; rather, He is as He has praised Himself. To Him belongs all praise in this world and the Hereafter. His is the judgment, and to Him you will be returned. For His own sake and for His kindness to His servants, He deserves their praise, love, and contentment with Him. Glorified and exalted is He! He deserves praise for His intrinsic praiseworthy qualities and for His kindness to His servants. The latter is praise in gratitude, while the former is praise without qualification.

Elsewhere, we have mentioned the view that everything Allah has created is a blessing for His believing servants, for which He deserves their praise and gratitude, and that it is among His favors. Thus, near the end of Surah al-Najm, He says:

“Then which of your Lord’s favors do you doubt?”

In Surah al-Rahmān, He mentions:

“Everyone on earth will perish,”

and similar statements, then says immediately afterward:

“Then which of your Lord’s favors will you both deny?”

Others, including al-Zajjāj and Abū al-Faraj ibn al-Jawzī, said concerning “Then which of your Lord’s favors will you both deny?” that this refers to the things mentioned. Through all of them He bestows blessings upon you: they point you to His oneness, and through them He provides you with what sustains you. They offered this explanation concerning Surah al-Rahmān.

---

## Page 7365

Concerning His words,

“Then which of your Lord’s favors do you doubt?”

they said: “Which of your Lord’s blessings, which point to His oneness, do you doubt?” Another explanation is: “Do you doubt and dispute?” Ibn ‘Abbās said: “Do you deny?”

I say: The expression tatamārā (“you dispute”) has been given the additional sense of tukadhdhibu (“you deny”). Hence it is made transitive with the letter tā. For tamārī (mutual disputation) is the tafā‘ul form derived from mirā’ (disputation). One says, “We disputed over the crescent moon.” Disputing concerning the Quran is disbelief, and it can involve denial and casting doubt.

It may also be said that, since the address was directed to them, He said tatamārā, meaning tatamārawna (“you [plural] dispute”), rather than tamtarī (“you doubt”). For the tafā‘ul form denotes an action shared by two parties who dispute with one another.

They said that the address is to the human being; according to one view, it is to al-Walīd ibn al-Mughīrah. For He said:

“Or has he not been informed of what is in the Scriptures of Moses, and of Abraham, who fulfilled his obligations—that no bearer of burdens shall bear the burden of another?”

Then He turned to address him, saying:

“Then which of your Lord’s favors do you doubt?”

That is: “Do you deny?” Similarly, He said:

“He created man from dry clay like pottery, and created the jinn from a smokeless flame of fire. Then which of your Lord’s favors will you both deny?”

Everything Allah has created thus contains kindness toward His servants, for which He is praised with the praise of gratitude. In it He also has a wise purpose that relates back to Him, for which He deserves the praise due to Him in His own right.

Thus all created things contain blessings for His servants, including the two communities of humans and jinn addressed in His words:

---

## Page 7366

“Then which of your Lord’s favors will you both deny?”

in that they are signs of the Lord through which people attain guidance and faith, bringing them happiness in this world and the Hereafter. They point them to Him and to His oneness, power, knowledge, wisdom, and mercy.

The signs with which He sent the prophets, supported them, and granted them victory, together with His destruction of their enemies—as He mentions in Surah al-Najm:

“And He destroyed ancient ‘Ād, and Thamūd, sparing none, and before them the people of Noah, who were indeed more unjust and more rebellious. And He cast down the overturned city, covering it with whatever covered it”—

demonstrate to them the truthfulness of the prophets in what they conveyed of commands and prohibitions, promises and threats, and the glad tidings and warnings they brought. Hence He said immediately afterward:

“This is a warner from among the warners of old.”

It is said that this refers to Muhammad, and it is said that it refers to the Quran. Allah has described each as a bearer of glad tidings and a warner. Concerning the Messenger of Allah, He said:

“I am only a warner and a bearer of glad tidings to people who believe.”

And He, the Exalted, said:

“Indeed, We have sent you as a witness, a bearer of glad tidings, and a warner.”

Concerning the Quran, He, the Exalted, said:

“A Book whose verses have been explained in detail—an Arabic Quran for people who know—bringing glad tidings and warnings.”

The two are inseparably linked, and both meanings are intended. It may be explained thus: this is a warner who has warned of what the earlier messengers and scriptures warned of. His words “from among the warners” mean that he is of their kind—that is, a messenger among

---

## Page 7367

the messengers who were sent.

Created things thus contain blessings, in that through them people attain guidance and faith, draw lessons, and take heed. These are the greatest blessings, for the greatest blessing is faith. Every created thing is a sign through which this blessing is attained. He, the Exalted, said:

“Indeed, in their stories is a lesson for people of understanding.”

And He, the Exalted, said:

“As insight and a reminder for every servant who turns to Him.”

Whatever befalls a person, if it pleases him, is an evident blessing. If it distresses him, it is nevertheless a blessing because it expiates his sins, he is rewarded for bearing it patiently, and it contains wisdom and mercy of which he is unaware:

“Perhaps you dislike something which is good for you, and perhaps you love something which is bad for you. Allah knows, and you do not know.”

It is also stated in the hadith: “By Allah, Allah decrees nothing for the believer unless it is

---

## Page 7368

good for him. If prosperity comes his way, he gives thanks, and that is good for him. If adversity befalls him, he is patient, and that is good for him.”

If he experiences either of these, both are among Allah’s blessings upon him. Both blessings require patience as well as gratitude. The need for patience in the blessing of adversity is clear. As for the blessing of prosperity, it requires perseverance in obedience to Allah amid that prosperity.

Indeed, the trial of prosperity is greater than the trial of adversity. As some of the early generations said: “We were tested with adversity and remained patient; we were tested with prosperity and failed to remain patient.” It is stated in the hadith:

“I seek refuge in You from the trial of poverty and the evil of the trial of wealth.”

Many people remain upright in poverty, whereas only a smaller number remain upright in wealth. This is why most of those who enter Paradise are poor: the trial of poverty is easier to bear. Both conditions require patience and gratitude. However, because prosperity brings pleasure and adversity brings pain, gratitude is commonly associated with prosperity, and patience with adversity. Allah, the Exalted, said:

“And if We let man taste mercy from Us and then withdraw it from him, he is indeed despairing and ungrateful.”

“And if We let him taste prosperity after adversity has touched him, he will surely say, ‘Hardships have left me.’

---

## Page 7369

He is indeed exultant and boastful.”

“Except for those who are patient and do righteous deeds; they will have forgiveness and a great reward.”

Another reason is that a person enjoying prosperity has greater need of gratitude, while a person facing adversity has greater need of patience. Patience is obligatory for the latter, and gratitude for the former. Whoever neglects this obligation deserves punishment.

As for patience in prosperity, it may be recommended when it involves refraining from excessive indulgence in desires. It may also be obligatory. Yet because of the gratitude he shows—which constitutes good deeds—Allah forgives some of his sins.

Likewise, for a person facing adversity, gratitude is not recommended when it is gratitude through which he becomes one of the foremost, those brought near to Allah. His shortcomings in gratitude may be forgiven on account of the patience he exercises.

Patience and gratitude come together when a person experiences both pain and pleasure: he bears the pain patiently and gives thanks for the blessings. This is a state that many people find difficult to attain. A fuller discussion of this belongs elsewhere.

The point here is that Allah, the Exalted, bestows blessings through all of this, even though most people do not initially perceive it as a blessing. Allah knows, whereas you do not know. Everything Allah does is therefore a blessing from Him.

As for a person’s sins, they come from himself. Yet even these are—with

---

## Page 7370

a good outcome—a blessing. They are also a blessing for others, through the lessons, guidance, and faith that others gain from them. Hence, among the finest supplications is:

“O Allah, do not make me a cautionary example for others, and do not let anyone benefit more than I do from what You have taught me.”

Among the supplications in the Quran are:

“Our Lord, do not make us a trial for the wrongdoing people.”

“Do not make us a trial for those who disbelieve.”

Likewise, it says:

“And make us leaders for the righteous.”

That is: make us leaders for those who follow our example and take us as their guides, and do not make us a trial for those who go astray through us and suffer misery.

The Arabic word ālā’ means blessings, and it also encompasses power. Ibn Qutaybah said:

“When Allah enumerated His blessings in this surah—Surah al-Rahmān—reminded His servants of His favors, and drew their attention to His power, He placed each such utterance as a refrain between two blessings, to make those blessings understood and bring people to acknowledge them.”

Al-Hākim, in his Sahīh, and al-Tirmidhī narrated from Jābir a report from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, recited Surah al-Rahmān to us until he completed it. Then he said: ‘Why do I see you remaining silent? The jinn responded better than you did. Not once did I recite to them this verse—“Then which of your Lord’s favors will you both deny?”—without their saying: “None of Your blessings, our Lord, do we deny. To You belongs all praise.”’”

---

## Page 7371

In the Quran, Allah Most High draws attention to His signs that attest to His power and lordship, to His signs that manifest His blessings and kindness toward His servants, and to His signs that reveal His wisdom. These are all inseparably connected. Everything He has created is a blessing and evidence of His power and wisdom.

Yet the blessing of provision and the benefits derived from food, drink, dwellings, and clothing are apparent to everyone. Hence, they are cited as evidence, as in Surah al-Nahl, which is also called the Surah of Blessings, as Qatādah and others have stated.

Accordingly, many people say that praise is broader than gratitude in terms of its grounds, since praise may be offered for a blessing or for something other than a blessing. Gratitude, however, is broader in its forms, since it may be expressed through the heart, the tongue, and the hand.

If, then, every created thing contains a blessing, praise can only be offered for a blessing. Praise be to Allah in every circumstance, for there is no circumstance He decrees that is not a blessing for His servants. This, however, is understood only by those who recognize the blessings contained in created things. The Jahmiyyah and the Jabriyyah are far removed from this understanding.

---

## Page 7372

Likewise, everything He creates contains wisdom on His part, and He is praised for it on account of that wisdom. The Jahmiyyah are also far removed from this understanding.

So too are the Qadariyyah, who say that the wisdom in His acts does not relate back to Him; rather, it concerns only the benefit of creation. Thus, they recognize only gratitude, just as the Jahmiyyah recognize only power.

Power devoid of blessing and wisdom displays no praiseworthy quality. This is like someone who has power but does something that benefits neither himself nor anyone else: such a person is not praised.

The position of the Jahmiyyah, the followers of Jahm, therefore amounts to saying that He does not deserve praise. In their view, He possesses dominion without praise, although even their understanding of His dominion is deficient. Conversely, the Mu‘tazilah attribute to Him a kind of praise without complete dominion, since, according to them, He wills things that do not occur, things occur that He does not will, and events take place without His power.

According to the way of the early Muslim generations, dominion and praise belong to Him in their entirety. He is praised for His wisdom just as He is praised for His power and mercy.

---

## Page 7373

He has said:

“Allah bears witness that there is no deity but Him, as do the angels and those endowed with knowledge. He upholds justice. There is no deity but Him, the Almighty, the All-Wise.”

He alone is worthy of worship, and to Him belong justice, might, and wisdom. Only the early Muslim generations and their followers affirm all four. Whoever falls short in his knowledge of the Sunnah has denied the Lord some of His due. The Jahmī determinist affirms neither justice nor wisdom nor Allah’s exclusive right to worship, but only His oneness in lordship.

Likewise, the Muʿtazilī does not truly affirm Allah’s exclusive right to worship, justice with regard to good and evil, or, in reality, might or wisdom—even if he claims to affirm wisdom whose purpose concerns someone other than Him. Yet that cannot constitute wisdom: someone who acts not for any purpose relating to himself, but for another, is, in the judgment of all rational people, not wise in doing so, but foolish.

If praise is offered only for a blessing, then it has been established that it is the head of gratitude. It is therefore gratitude’s beginning. And although praise is for both His blessings and His wisdom, gratitude expressed through deeds

---

## Page 7374

is for His blessings. It is also worship of Him in recognition of His exclusive right to worship, which encompasses His wisdom. Thus all these matters fall within gratitude. This is why the Quran places such emphasis on gratitude, but not on praise in isolation, since praise is a form of gratitude. Praise—which is gratitude expressed in words—has been prescribed at the beginning of every address, together with affirming Allah’s oneness.

Al-Fātihah contains gratitude and the affirmation of Allah’s oneness. The prescribed sermons must likewise include both. The enduring righteous deeds are of two kinds: “Glory and praise be to Allah” comprises gratitude, declaring Him free of imperfection, and exaltation; “There is no deity but Allah” and “Allah is the Greatest” comprise affirming His oneness and proclaiming His greatness. Allah Most High says:

“So call upon Him with sincere devotion to Him. All praise is for Allah, Lord of all worlds.”

Is praise offered for everything for which someone is commended, even if it is not of his own choosing? Or is praise restricted to matters involving choice, as has been said of blame? This calls for examination, but this is not the place for it.

It is reported in the Sahīh that when the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, raised his head from bowing, he would say:

“Our Lord, to You belongs all praise, enough to fill the heaven and to fill

---

## Page 7375

the earth, and to fill whatever else You will. You are worthy of praise and glory. This is the truest thing a servant can say—and we are all Your servants. None can withhold what You give, and none can give what You withhold; nor can wealth avail its possessor against You.” This is the wording of the hadith.

“The truest” (ahaqqu) is a superlative. Some authors have mistakenly given the wording as, “True is what the servant says.” This is not the Messenger’s wording, nor is it a sound statement, for a servant speaks both truth and falsehood. Rather, what the Lord says is true, as He Most High says:

“The truth—and I speak the truth.”

The actual wording, however, is “the truest thing a servant can say.” This is the predicate of an omitted subject, meaning: “Praise is the truest thing a servant can say,” or, “This—namely, praise—is the truest thing a servant can say.”

This makes clear that praising Allah is the truest thing His servants can say. For this reason, He has made it obligatory to utter it in every prayer and has ordained that al-Fātihah begin with it. He has also made it obligatory to utter it in every sermon and in every matter of importance. Praise is the opposite of blame. Praise concerns the praiseworthy qualities of the one praised and is accompanied by love for him, just as blame concerns his faults and is accompanied by hatred for him.

Thus, if it is said that He, glorified be He, does good and acts benevolently, and that He is Wise and Merciful

---

## Page 7376

toward His servants, more merciful to them than a mother is to her child, then this necessarily calls for His servants to love and praise Him.

But suppose it is said instead: “Rather, He creates what is pure evil, containing no benefit, mercy, or wisdom for anyone. He is characterized only by a will that favors one of two equivalent alternatives over the other. To Him, there is no difference between showing mercy and inflicting punishment. Neither His very being nor His will favors kindness toward creation; rather, tormenting them and granting them bliss are equal in His sight. Moreover, He creates what He creates solely for torment and evil, and does what He does without any wise purpose”—and other such claims made by the Jahmiyyah. This would not call for His servants to love and praise Him. Rather, it would call for the opposite.

For this reason, many of these people utter words of blame, abuse, and disparagement, expressing them in both poetry and prose. Many of their shaykhs and scholars say things that imply this. Those who do not voice it have hearts filled with it, but they see no benefit in expressing it or fear the Muslim public. Similar sentiments appear in the poetry of a number of these shaykhs. These people advance the arguments of Iblīs and his followers against Allah and portray the Lord as unjust toward them.

---

## Page 7377

This contradicts what Allah has said of Himself in His words, Most High:

“We did not wrong them, but it was they who were the wrongdoers.”

And His words:

“We did not wrong them, but they wronged themselves.”

And His words:

“Your Lord is never unjust to His servants.”

How could He be unjust? In their own dealings, if one person wronged another or failed to give him his due, the latter would hold him accountable, punish him, and exact retribution. This would be just, provided he did not transgress against him.

If the offender were to say, “What I did was decreed for me, so I bear no blame for it,” all rational people agree that this would not be accepted among them as an excuse. If rational people agree that a created being’s rights cannot be set aside by appealing to divine decree, how can the Creator’s rights be set aside by appealing to divine decree? He—glory be to Him—is the just Judge, who does not wrong anyone even by an atom’s weight. If there is a good deed, He multiplies it and grants a great reward from Himself. This has been discussed at length elsewhere.

Thus, the statement, “The most fitting words a servant can say,” implies that praising Allah is the most fitting thing a servant can say. To Him belongs all praise in every circumstance, for He does only what is good

---

## Page 7378

and beneficent, for which He—glory be to Him, Most High—deserves praise, even if His servants do not understand. He—glory be to Him—created man and created his soul with a natural impulse to act, one that inevitably involves some evil, out of consummate wisdom and boundless mercy.

If it is asked, “Why, then, did He not create it differently?” the answer is: “That would have been a creation other than man, and the wise purpose He brought about through creating man would not have been achieved.” This was the angels’ question when they said:

“Will You place upon it one who causes corruption therein and sheds blood?”

How could individual human beings know what even the angels did not know? The human soul was created as Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, man was created anxious: distressed when evil touches him, and withholding when good touches him.”

And He Most High says:

“Man was created hasty.”

Thus, the soul was created with a constitution that necessarily gives rise to what has arisen from it, out of great wisdom and all-embracing mercy. This, then, was goodness and mercy, even though it involved relative evil, as explained earlier. This addresses the matter in terms of its ultimate purpose, although evil is not attributed to Allah.

As for the second aspect, that of the cause: this evil arose only because of the absence of

---

## Page 7379

the knowledge and will that set the soul right. For it was created with an innate disposition that calls for knowing and loving Allah, and it has been guided to knowledge and deeds that help it achieve this. All of this comes from Allah’s grace and beneficence. But when the sinful soul found no one to perfect it, and instead encountered devils among mankind and jinn who made evil deeds seem attractive to it, it inclined toward them and committed evil deeds. Its commission of evil thus resulted from a combination of the absence of what benefits it and is best for it, and the presence of those who bewildered it. Absence is not attributed to Allah.
