“Then, when adversity touches you, to Him you cry out for help.”

This describes their condition. Crying out entails raising one’s voice, and a person only cries out when adversity afflicts him. When enjoying blessings, however, he is quiet, whether grateful or ungrateful.

“Then, when adversity touches you, to Him you cry out for help.”

“Then, when He removes the adversity from you, some of you at once associate partners with their Lord.”

---

## Page 7433

And Allah has mentioned this meaning in several places, condemning those who associate partners with Him after He has relieved their affliction and lavished blessings upon them. The servant then attributes those blessings to someone other than Him, worships someone other than Him, the Most High, and thanks someone else for those blessings. As Allah Most High says:

“When adversity touches people, they call upon their Lord, turning to Him in repentance. Then, when He gives them a taste of mercy from Him, some of them at once associate partners with their Lord, showing ingratitude for what We have given them. So enjoy yourselves; you will soon know.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Say, ‘Who rescues you from the darkness of the land and sea, when you call upon Him humbly and secretly, saying: If He rescues us from this, we will surely be among the grateful?’ Say, ‘Allah rescues you from it and from every distress; yet you still associate partners with Him.’”

And Allah Most High says:

“When adversity touches man, he calls upon his Lord, turning to Him in repentance. Then, when He bestows a blessing upon him from Himself, he forgets what he was praying about before and sets up rivals to Allah to lead others astray from His path. Say, ‘Enjoy your disbelief for a little while; you are indeed among the people of the Fire.’”

His words, “He forgets what he was praying about,” mean that he forgets the adversity he had been calling upon Allah to remove, as He says in Surah al-An‘ām:

“Say, ‘Tell me: if Allah’s punishment came upon you, or the Hour came upon you, would you call upon anyone other than Allah, if you are truthful?’ No! It is Him alone you would call upon, and, if He willed, He would remove the affliction about which you called upon Him, and you would forget the partners you associate with Him.”

Thus Allah, glorified be He, condemns two groups: those who neither call upon Him in adversity nor turn to Him in repentance; and those who call upon Him, humbly implore Him, and turn to Him in repentance. Then, when

---

## Page 7434

He relieves their affliction, they turn away from Him and associate with Him the rivals they have taken besides Him. Thus these people are of two kinds—like the negators and the polytheists. One group, when affliction befalls them, neither call upon Allah, nor humbly implore Him, nor turn to Him in repentance, as He says:

“We certainly sent messengers to nations before you, then afflicted them with hardship and adversity so that they might humbly implore Us.”

“Why, then, did they not humbly implore Us when Our punishment came upon them? Instead, their hearts grew hard, and Satan made their deeds seem fair to them.”

Allah Most High also says:

“We certainly seized them with punishment, yet they did not humble themselves before their Lord, nor do they humbly implore Him.”

And Allah Most High says:

“Do they not see that they are tested once or twice every year? Yet they neither repent nor take heed.”

And Allah Most High says:

“We will surely make them taste the lesser punishment before the greater punishment, so that they may return.”

The other group humbly implore Him in adversity and turn to Him in repentance. But when He relieves their affliction, they turn away from Him, as Allah Most High says:

“When affliction touches man, he calls upon Us while lying on his side, sitting, or standing. But when We relieve his affliction, he goes on as though he had never called upon Us concerning an affliction that touched him. Thus the deeds of the transgressors are made to seem fair to them.”

And Allah Most High says:

“When We bestow blessings upon man, he turns away and withdraws; but when evil touches him, he prays at length.”

And Allah Most High says:

“When affliction touches you at sea, all those you call upon are forgotten except Him. But when He delivers you safely to land, you turn away. Man is ever ungrateful.”

And He says of the polytheists, as cited earlier:

“Then, when affliction touches you, it is to Him that you cry out.”

“Then, when He removes

---

## Page 7435

the affliction from you, some of you associate partners with their Lord.”

The praiseworthy group is the third: those who call upon Him and repent to Him, remaining steadfast in worshipping Him and repenting to Him in times of ease. They worship and obey Him in both prosperity and adversity. These are the people of patience and gratitude, as He has related concerning His prophets, peace be upon them. Allah Most High says:

“And remember Jonah, when he went away in anger, thinking that We would not constrain him. Then he called out in the darkness, ‘There is no god but You. Glory be to You! I have indeed been among the wrongdoers.’”

“So We answered him and delivered him from distress. Thus do We deliver the believers.”

Allah Most High also says:

“We certainly tested Solomon and cast upon his throne a body; then he turned to Us in repentance.”

“He said, ‘My Lord, forgive me and grant me a kingdom such as will not belong to anyone after me. You alone are the Bestower.’”

Allah Most High also says:

“Has the account of the disputants reached you, when they scaled the wall of the prayer chamber?”

“When they entered David’s presence, he was alarmed by them. They said, ‘Do not be afraid. We are two disputing parties, one of whom has wronged the other. Judge between us in truth, do not be unjust, and guide us to the right way.’”

“‘This is my brother. He has ninety-nine ewes, while I have only one. Yet he said, “Entrust her to me,” and he overpowered me in argument.’”

“He said, ‘He has certainly wronged you by demanding your ewe in addition to his own. Indeed, many partners wrong one another, except those who believe and do righteous deeds—and how few they are!’ Then David realized that We had tested him, so he sought his Lord’s forgiveness, fell down bowing, and turned to Him in repentance.”

“So We forgave him for that. Indeed, he will have nearness to Us and an excellent place of return.”

Allah Most High also says concerning Adam and Eve:

“So he lured them through deception. Then, when they tasted

---

## Page 7436

the tree, their nakedness became apparent to them, and they began covering themselves with leaves from Paradise. Their Lord called out to them, ‘Did I not forbid you that tree and tell you that Satan is a clear enemy to you?’”

“They said, ‘Our Lord, we have wronged ourselves. If You do not forgive us and have mercy upon us, we will surely be among the losers.’”

He also says:

“Then Adam received words from his Lord, and He accepted his repentance. Indeed, He is the Accepter of repentance, the Most Merciful.”

Allah Most High says concerning the believers whose prophet was killed:

“How many a prophet fought with many followers beside him! They did not lose heart because of what befell them in Allah’s cause, nor did they weaken or submit. And Allah loves the patient.”

“Their only words were, ‘Our Lord, forgive us our sins and our excesses in our affairs, make our feet firm, and grant us victory over the disbelieving people.’”

“So Allah gave them the reward of this world and the excellent reward of the Hereafter. And Allah loves those who do good.”

His words, “was killed,” mean that the prophet was killed. This is the more correct of the two interpretations. His words, “with many followers beside him,” constitute a clause in the predicate position, qualifying the prophet—one qualification following another. That is: how many a prophet who had many followers was killed, while they were not killed with him.

For the meaning would then have been that he was killed while they were with him. What is intended is that he had many followers and, in general terms, that he was killed. Those followers “did not lose heart because of what befell them in Allah’s cause, nor did they weaken or submit.”

---

## Page 7437

And “ribbiyyūn” means great multitudes—many thousands.

This meaning accords with the occasion of revelation: what befell them on the day of Uhud when it was said, “Muhammad has been killed.” Earlier, Allah had said:

“Muhammad is no more than a messenger; messengers have passed away before him. If he dies or is killed, will you turn back on your heels? Whoever turns back on his heels will not harm Allah at all. And Allah will reward the grateful.”

This is the verse that Abū Bakr al-Siddīq, may Allah be pleased with him, recited on the day the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, died. He said, “Whoever worshipped Muhammad, Muhammad has died. But whoever worships Allah, Allah is Ever-Living and never dies.”

When a prophet is killed or dies, people—believers and disbelievers alike—face a tremendous trial. Apostasy and hypocrisy arise because his followers’ hearts falter at his death, and because Satan instils in the disbelievers’ hearts such thoughts as: this man’s cause has come to an end, and his religion can no longer endure; had he been a prophet, he would not have been killed or defeated; and other thoughts of this kind.

Allah Most High thus tells us how many a prophet was killed. The Children of Israel indeed killed many prophets. Such a prophet had many followers with him. He might have been killed outside any war or battle. Rather, he could be killed even though great multitudes had followed him. Yet the believers did not lose heart because of what befell them through his killing, nor did they weaken or submit. And Allah loves the patient. But they sought forgiveness for their sins, on account of which

---

## Page 7438

calamities occur—for whatever adversity befell them came from themselves—and they asked Allah to forgive them and make their feet firm, keeping them steadfast in faith and jihad so that they would neither fall into doubt nor shrink from jihad. Allah Most High says:

“The believers are only those who believe in Allah and His Messenger, then harbour no doubt, and strive with their wealth and their lives in the cause of Allah. It is they who are the truthful.”

They also asked Him to grant them victory over the disbelieving people. They asked their Lord both to make them steadfast within themselves and to bestow victory upon them from Himself. For He alone grants victory, and victory comes only from Allah. Likewise, He sent down the angels to assist them. Allah Most High said concerning His sending down the angels:

“Allah made it only as good news and to reassure your hearts thereby. Victory comes only from Allah. Indeed, Allah is Almighty, All-Wise.”

Allah Most High also says:

“So Allah gave them the reward of this world and the excellent reward of the Hereafter. And Allah loves those who do good.”

This is discussed at length elsewhere.

The point here is that, since good comes from His kindness, Most High, while calamities arise from a person’s own self—even though they occur by Allah’s decree and predestination—the servant must thank his Lord, glory be to Him, seek His forgiveness for his sins, and rely upon Him alone. For none but He brings good things.

This therefore requires the servant to affirm His oneness, rely upon Him alone, thank Him alone, and seek forgiveness for sins.

---

## Page 7439

The Prophet (may Allah bless him and grant him peace) used to bring all these matters together in prayer. It is authentically reported that, when he raised his head from bowing, he would say:

“Our Lord, to You belongs all praise, filling heaven, filling the earth, filling all that lies between them, and filling whatever else You will. You are worthy of praise and glory—the most fitting words a servant can utter, and we are all Your servants.”

This is praise, which is gratitude to Allah Most High, and a declaration that praising Him is the most fitting thing a servant can say. He would then say:

“O Allah, none can withhold what You give, and none can give what You withhold; nor can the good fortune of the fortunate avail them against You.”

This affirms His oneness: His oneness in lordship—in creation, decree, origination, and guidance. He alone gives and withholds: none can withhold what He gives, and none can give what He withholds. It also affirms His sole right to worship—in legislation, commands, and prohibitions. This means that even if people are granted dominion, greatness, good fortune, and leadership, whether outwardly or inwardly—as with those who experience unveilings and exercise extraordinary powers—

“the good fortune of the fortunate cannot avail them against You.”

That is, a person’s good fortune, greatness, and wealth cannot save or deliver him from Your questioning and reckoning. Hence he said, “cannot avail him against You,” rather than, “cannot avail him with You.” Had the latter been said, it might have suggested that although these things do not bring him nearer to You, they might not harm him either. The fortunate person might then say: “If I escape punishment in the Hereafter, I do not care”—like those who

---

## Page 7440

were granted prophethood and kingship: they possessed worldly dominion and were among the blessed. A fortunate person who comes after such a one, but has not acted in obedience to Allah, may imagine that the same applies to him. Thus he said:

“Nor can good fortune avail the fortunate against You.”

Here, “avail” carries the meaning of “save and deliver,” making clear that his good fortune cannot save him from punishment. Rather, his sins make him deserve what others like him deserve. His good fortune cannot avail him against You: it neither saves nor delivers him. These words thus fully affirm Allah’s oneness and give full expression to His words:

“You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help.”

And His words:

“So worship Him and put your trust in Him.”

And His words:

“In Him I put my trust, and to Him I turn.”

And His words:

“Remember the name of your Lord and devote yourself wholly to Him.”

“Lord of the East and the West; there is no god but Him, so take Him as your Trustee.”

His saying, “None can withhold what You give, and none can give what You withhold,” affirms Allah’s oneness in lordship. This entails that He—glorified is He—is the One to whom requests and supplications are addressed and in whom trust is placed. It leads to affirming His sole right to worship and serves as evidence for it, as it is used in the Quran to argue against the polytheists. For the polytheists acknowledged this oneness—His oneness in lordship—yet still associated partners with Allah. They set up rivals to Him, loving them as they loved Allah.

They say that these are their intercessors with Him and that through them they draw near to Him. Thus they take them as intercessors and as means of drawing near, as Allah Most High says: “They worship besides Allah that which neither

---

## Page 7441

harms them nor benefits them, and they say, ‘These are our intercessors with Allah.’” And Allah Most High says:

“Those who take protectors besides Him say, ‘We worship them only so that they may bring us nearer to Allah.’”

And Allah Most High says:

“We certainly destroyed the towns around you and explained Our signs in various ways so that they might return.”

“Why, then, did those whom they had taken as gods besides Allah, as a means of drawing near to Him, not help them? Rather, they were lost to them. Such was their falsehood and what they used to fabricate.”

This affirmation of Allah’s oneness means worshipping Allah alone, without any partner, and worshipping Him only in ways that He loves and approves: what He has commanded and prescribed through His messengers—may Allah’s blessings be upon them. It therefore includes obeying Him and His Messenger, showing loyalty to His allies and enmity toward His enemies, and loving Allah and His Messenger more than anyone or anything else.

It also entails loving Allah with a love in which no one else equals or rivals Him. Indeed, it requires that His Messenger—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—be dearer to a person than his own self. If the Messenger, because he is Allah’s Messenger, must be dearer to the believer than his own self, then how much more so his Lord, glorified and exalted is He?

Sahīh al-Bukhārī reports that ʿUmar said, “O Messenger of Allah, by Allah, you are dearer to me than everything except my own self.” He replied, “No, ʿUmar, not until I am

---

## Page 7442

dearer to you than your own self.” He said, “By the One who sent you with the truth, you are indeed dearer to me than my own self.” He replied, “Now, Umar.”

Allah Most High has said:

“The Prophet has a greater claim over the believers than they have over themselves.”

And Allah Most High says:

“Say, ‘If your fathers, your sons, your brothers, your spouses, your relatives, the wealth you have acquired, the trade whose decline you fear, and the homes you delight in are dearer to you than Allah and His Messenger and striving in His cause, then wait until Allah brings about His command. And Allah does not guide the defiantly disobedient people.’”

If Allah, His Messenger, and striving in His cause are not dearer to a servant than his family and wealth of every kind, then he falls under this warning.

This affirmation of Allah’s oneness—His oneness in worship—entails doing what is commanded and refraining from what is forbidden. This includes patiently enduring what has been decreed, just as the former entails acknowledging that none creates, provides, gives, or withholds except Allah alone. It therefore requires that the servant ask no one but Him, rely on none but Him, and seek help from none but Him. As Allah Most High says concerning both kinds:

“You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help.”

And He says:

“So worship Him and put your trust in Him.”

---

## Page 7443

And this affirmation of Allah’s oneness is what distinguishes those who worship Him alone from those who associate partners with Him. It is the basis of recompense and reward in this life and the Hereafter. Whoever fails to fulfil it is among the polytheists who will remain in Hell forever. For Allah does not forgive associating partners with Him, but He forgives anything less than that for whomever He wills.

As for affirming Allah’s oneness in lordship, the polytheists acknowledged it, yet they worshipped others alongside Allah and loved them as they loved Him. Thus, that affirmation of His oneness—His oneness in lordship—was evidence against them. If Allah is the Lord and Sovereign of everything, and none creates or provides except Him, why do they worship another alongside Him, when that other has neither created nor provided for them and has no power to withhold anything from them or grant them anything? Rather, he is a servant like themselves, with no power even over his own harm or benefit, death, life, or resurrection.

If they say, “So that he may intercede,” Allah has said:

“Who is it that can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

Thus, even those angels and prophets who are granted intercession cannot intercede except by His permission. As for their graves—and the domes and monuments erected over them—or representations made in their likeness, whether three-dimensional or pictorial, to regard seeking intercession through these as seeking intercession through those persons is invalid according to both reason and revelation. These things cannot intercede under any circumstances, nor can any of the other idols made to represent celestial bodies, jinn, righteous people, or others.

---

## Page 7444

Since no one can intercede with Allah except by His permission, and they intercede only for those He approves, intercessors are not partners with Him, unlike intercession by one created being with another. A created being may receive intercession from an equal—or someone above or below him—without having given the intercessor permission.

The one petitioned is bound to accept the intercession: either because he desires the intercessor’s favour, or seeks the benefit of some power or means at his disposal through which he can help him or avert what he fears; because he fears him; because he loves him; because of their mutual exchange of favours and assistance; or for some other reason.

It is the intercessor’s intervention that moves the will of the one petitioned, making him wish to grant the intercession when previously he had not wished to do so. This is like a command that influences the person commanded, so that he carries it out when previously he had not intended to do so. Likewise, a request made by one created being to another may move the latter to do what is asked of him.

Thus, just as the intercessor joins the person seeking his intercession in making the request, he also joins the one being petitioned: through his intercession, the latter comes to carry out what is requested. He has thus joined both the petitioner and the one petitioned. But Allah Most High is One, without a second; no one can make Him one of a pair. Thus no one intercedes with Him except by His permission.

---

## Page 7445

Thus all authority belongs to Him alone; He has no partner in any respect. For this reason, He—glorified be He—negates such intercession in the Verse of the Throne, which affirms His oneness. He says:

“Allah—there is no deity except Him, the Ever-Living, the Sustainer of all. Neither drowsiness overtakes Him nor sleep. To Him belongs whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth. Who can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

On the Day of Resurrection, when the foremost of intercessors—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—prostrates himself and praises his Lord, he is told:

“‘Raise your head; speak and you will be heard; ask and you will be granted what you ask; intercede and your intercession will be accepted.’ A limit is then set for him, and he admits them to Paradise.”

All authority belongs to Allah, as He says:

“Say, ‘Indeed, all authority belongs to Allah.’”

He also says to His Messenger:

“You have no share in the decision.”

And He says:

“Unquestionably, His is the creation and the command.”

Since no one can intercede with Allah except by His permission, He grants permission to whomever He wills; nevertheless, the intercessor is honoured by having his intercession accepted. As the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said in an authentic hadith:

“Intercede and you will be rewarded, and Allah decrees through the words of His Prophet whatever He wills.”

When someone supplicates to Him and an intercessor intercedes with Him, and He hears the supplication and accepts the intercession, this does not exert an influence upon Him as one created being influences another. For He—glorified be He—is the One who causes the one to supplicate and the other to intercede. He is the Creator of His servants’ deeds. He is the One who enables His servant to repent and then accepts his repentance; who enables him to perform a deed and then rewards him for it; and who enables him to supplicate and then answers him. Thus nothing exerts any influence upon Him

---

## Page 7446

from among created beings. Rather, He—glorified be He—is the One who makes one of His actions a cause for another of His actions. This accords with the principles of Ahl al-Sunnah, who believe in divine decree: that Allah is the Creator of everything; that whatever He wills comes to pass, and whatever He does not will does not come to pass; and that nothing occurs except by His will. He is the Creator of His servants’ deeds, just as He is the Creator of all other created things.

Yahyā ibn Saʿīd al-Qattān said:

“I have always heard our companions say, ‘Allah is the Creator of His servants’ deeds.’”

This, however, contradicts the position of the Qadariyyah. For if they hold that the servant himself brings his deeds into existence and creates them independently of Allah’s will and creation, it follows that the servant has made his Lord perform an act He was not performing. Through his supplication, he has made Him answer him; through his repentance, he has made Him accept repentance; and through his intercession, he has made Him accept intercession.

This resembles the position of those who hold that a created being intercedes with Allah without His permission. For “permission” is of two kinds: permission in the sense of willing and creating, and permission in the sense of allowing and authorizing.

An example of the first is His statement concerning sorcery:

“They do not harm anyone through it except by Allah’s permission.”

For this occurs by Allah’s will and power; He has not, however, permitted sorcery.

---

## Page 7447

The Qadariyyah deny this “permission.” What their position really amounts to is that sorcery causes harm without Allah’s permission. Likewise, He says:

“Whatever befell you on the day the two armies met was by Allah’s permission.”

For if the killing, wounding, mutilation, and defeat that befell them occurred by His permission, then He is the Creator of both the disbelievers’ deeds and the believers’ deeds.

The second kind is illustrated by His statement:

“Indeed, We have sent you as a witness, a bearer of glad tidings, and a warner, and as one who calls to Allah by His permission.”

And His statement:

“Whatever palm trees you cut down or left standing on their roots, it was by Allah’s permission.”

This entails His allowing and authorizing that action and absolving its doer of blame and sin, while it also occurs by His will and decree. Thus, His statement:

“Who can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

refers to this permission, which accords with both His decree and His revealed law. He did not mean merely His will and decree, for sorcery and the disbelievers’ victory over the believers occurred by permission in that sense.

Thus, anyone who holds that people perform their deeds without Allah creating those deeds, having power over them, or willing them holds that every intercessor and supplicant has acted without Allah’s creation and power—even if He has permitted intercession.

As for disbelief, sorcery, and the fighting undertaken by the disbelievers, these, in their view, occur without His permission

---

## Page 7448

—neither in the one sense nor in the other. For, by the consensus of the Muslims, He has not permitted such acts. In their view, He neither willed nor created them; rather, they occurred without His will or creation.

The polytheists who acknowledge divine decree say that intercessors intercede by permission pertaining to His decree, even if He has not authorized or permitted them to do so. Those who deny divine decree—such as many Christians—say that intercessors intercede without permission, whether pertaining to His decree or His revealed law.

The Qadariyyah among the Muslims say that intercessors intercede without permission pertaining to His decree. Whoever petitions Allah without His permission under His revealed law has thus interceded with Him without permission pertaining either to His decree or to His revealed law.

Thus, in their view, a supplicant authorized to supplicate exerts an influence upon Allah, but with His authorization. As for an unauthorized supplicant, if Allah answers his supplication, then, in their view, he has influenced Him without either kind of permission—as in the supplication of Balʿām ibn Bāʿūrāʾ and others. Yet Allah Most High says:

“Who can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

If it is said, “Some intercessors intercede without Allah’s permission under His revealed law, even though

---

## Page 7449

He is the Creator of their act—as in Noah’s intercession for his son, Abraham’s intercession for his father, and the Prophet’s intercession—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—for ʿAbdullāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl when he offered the funeral prayer for him after his death. Concerning His words, ‘Who can intercede with Him except by His permission?’ you have said that they encompass both types of permission. Yet if He meant permission pertaining to His decree, every intercession would fall within this, just as every act of disbelief and sorcery does. There would then be no distinction between what occurs by His permission and what occurs without His permission. If He meant only permission under His revealed law, the position of the Qadariyyah would follow. Yet these individuals interceded without permission under His revealed law?”

The answer is: What is ruled out in the absence of permission is complete intercession—that is, intercession that is accepted. This is like the worshipper’s words, “Allah hears whoever praises Him,” meaning that He responds to him. Likewise, Allah Most High says:

“Guidance for the God-fearing.”

“You are only a warner to those who fear it.”

“So remind with the Quran those who fear My warning.”

The same applies to similar passages. For guidance, warning, reminding, and teaching require acceptance on the learner’s part. When he learns, the intended result of teaching has been achieved. Otherwise, one says, “I taught him, but he did not learn.” Likewise, it is said:

“As for Thamūd, We guided them, but they preferred blindness to guidance.”

The same applies to intercession. Its intended outcome is acceptance by the one to whom it is addressed: this is complete intercession. This is what cannot occur except by His permission. But if an intercessor intercedes and it is not accepted

---

## Page 7450

then his intercession is as though it had never occurred, and he must repent and seek forgiveness for it. As Noah said:

“My Lord, I seek refuge in You from asking You for that of which I have no knowledge. Unless You forgive me and have mercy on me, I will be among the losers.”

Likewise, Allah forbade the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—to offer funeral prayers for the hypocrites. He said to him:

“Never offer the funeral prayer for any of them who dies, nor stand at his grave. They disbelieved in Allah and His Messenger and died defiantly disobedient.”

He also said to him:

“It makes no difference to them whether you ask forgiveness for them or do not ask forgiveness for them: Allah will never forgive them.”

For this reason, He relates the words of the polytheists:

“So we have no intercessors,
nor any close friend.”

The intercession sought is that of someone whose request is heeded and whose intercession is accepted. No one possesses such intercession with Allah except by His permission, both in His decree and under His revealed law. He must permit it, and He must make His servant an intercessor. He is thus both the Creator of the servant’s act and the One who permits it. The same applies to someone who supplicates: Allah is the One who commands him to supplicate, and He is the One who makes him supplicate. Everything therefore belongs to Allah, both in creation and in command. As He says:

“Surely, His is the creation and the command.”

It is related in a hadith mentioned by Ibn Abī Hātim and others that he said:

“Let whoever is confident of possessing it lay claim to it.”

That is, no share in either creation or command remains for anyone else.

Now, since the intercession affirmed is intercession in the full sense—the intended purpose of intercession, namely that which is accepted, as opposed to that which is rejected—for no one desires the latter, neither

---

## Page 7451

the intercessor, nor the one on whose behalf intercession is made, nor the one to whom it is addressed. If the intercessor and the one on whose behalf intercession is made knew that it would be rejected, they would not pursue it.

Accepted intercession is that which brings benefit. He makes this clear in such statements as:

“Intercession will not benefit anyone with Him except those for whom He gives permission.”

And:

“On that Day, intercession will not benefit anyone except one to whom the Most Merciful gives permission and whose words He approves.”

He thus ruled out unrestricted intercession and made clear that intercession brings no benefit with Him except for those for whom He gives permission. This is permission under His revealed law, meaning that He allows and authorizes it, as in His words:

“Permission to fight has been given to those who are being fought, because they have been wronged.”

And:

“Do not enter the Prophet’s houses unless permission is given to you.”

And:

“Let those whom your right hands possess ask your permission.”

There are other similar examples. His words, “except those for whom He gives permission,” refer to permission concerning the one on whose behalf intercession is made. He does not give anyone permission to intercede without restriction. Rather, He permits them to intercede only for those on whose behalf He has authorized them to intercede. Allah Most High says:

“On that Day, they will follow the caller without deviation, and voices will be hushed before the Most Merciful, so you will hear nothing but a whisper. On that Day, intercession will not benefit anyone except one to whom the Most Merciful gives permission and whose words He approves.”

There are two interpretations of this. According to one, it means: except the intercession of one to whom the Most Merciful gives permission. According to the other, it means: intercession will benefit only one for whom the Most Merciful gives permission; he is the one who benefits from intercession. This is the interpretation given by a group of Quran commentators, who mention no other.

---

## Page 7452

because He did not say, “It benefits only one to whom He gives permission,” nor did He say, “Intercession brings benefit only in the case of one to whom He gives permission.” Rather, He said:

“Intercession benefits only one to whom He gives permission.”

Thus, intercession brings benefit, is benefited from, and proves beneficial only for those for whom permission has been given. As He Most High says in the other verse:

“Intercession will not benefit anyone with Him except those for whom He gives permission.”

It does not say, “It benefits only an intercessor who has been given permission.” Had this been intended, it would have said, “Intercession brings no benefit with Him except from one to whom He gives permission.” Instead, He says, “except for one to whom He gives permission,” meaning the person on whose behalf intercession is made and who benefits from it.

In His words, “Until, when fear is removed from their hearts,” the pronoun does not refer back to “the intercessors.” Rather, it refers to those mentioned in His words:

“They have no share in either, nor does He have any helper from among them.”

Then He says:

“Intercession will not benefit anyone with Him.”

He then makes clear that this is ruled out:

“Until, when fear is removed from their hearts, they say, ‘What has your Lord said?’ They say, ‘The truth.’”

They do not know what He has said until fear is removed from their hearts, so how could they intercede without His permission?

When He, glorified be He, gives permission concerning the person on whose behalf intercession is made, He has thereby given permission to the intercessor. This, then, is comprehensive permission, unlike permission given only to the intercessor: that does not necessarily mean that He has given permission concerning the person on whose behalf intercession is made, for He may give the intercessor a limited permission.

---

## Page 7453

Several Quran commentators have likewise said this. They stated: “This indicates that intercession benefits only the believers.” The early generations also explained this verse in the same way. Commenting on His words,

“except one to whom the Most Merciful gives permission and whose words He approves,”

Qatādah said: “The scholars used to say that the praiseworthy station mentioned in Allah Most High’s words, ‘Your Lord may raise you to a praiseworthy station,’ is his intercession on the Day of Resurrection. As for His words, ‘except one to whom the Most Merciful gives permission and whose words He approves,’ Allah accepts the believers’ intercession for one another.”

Al-Baghawī said concerning “except one to whom the Most Merciful gives permission”: “Allah has permitted intercession to be made on his behalf.” Concerning “and whose words He approves,” he said: “That is, He approves of what he says.” Ibn ʿAbbās said: “It means that he has said, ‘There is no god but Allah.’” Al-Baghawī said: “This indicates that intercession is made only on behalf of a believer.”

They mentioned both interpretations concerning His words, Most High:

“Intercession will not benefit anyone with Him except those for whom He gives permission.”

There, a group of commentators put first the interpretation that the exception refers to the intercessor, rather than the person on whose behalf intercession is made, unlike the interpretation they put first here. Among them was al-Baghawī, who here mentioned only the person on whose behalf intercession is made when explaining the exception.

---

## Page 7454

Elsewhere, he said concerning His words,

“Intercession will not benefit anyone with Him except those for whom He gives permission”:

“That is, permission to intercede. He said this to refute their claim, ‘These are our intercessors with Allah.’” He said: “It may also mean: except someone for whom He has permitted intercession to be made.”

They likewise mentioned both interpretations concerning His words:

“Those whom they invoke besides Him possess no power of intercession, except those who bear witness to the truth.”

We shall discuss this verse, if Allah Most High wills, and explain that the exception encompasses both groups and is a disconnected exception. These two verses convey the same meaning as that verse, encompassing both categories.

The explanation is that He, glorified be He, said:

“On that Day, intercession will benefit none except one to whom the Most Merciful gives permission and whose words He approves.”

“Intercession” is the verbal noun of “to intercede.” A verbal noun is sometimes placed in a possessive construction with the agent, and sometimes with the object of the action—the latter corresponding to what is sometimes termed the “direct object.” Thus one may say, “I admired the beating of the cloth,” or “the fuller's beating.”

The term “knowledge” is similarly related sometimes to knowledge and sometimes to what is known. Examples of the former are His words:

“They encompass nothing of His knowledge,”

and His words,

“He has sent it down with His knowledge,”

and His words,

“that it has only been sent down with Allah’s knowledge,”

and similar passages.

An example of the latter is His statement:

“Indeed, Allah has knowledge of the Hour.”

Here, the Hour is what is known, not the knower. Another example is His statement when Pharaoh said:

“What, then, of the former generations?”

---

## Page 7455

Moses said:

“Knowledge of them is with my Lord in a Record. My Lord neither errs nor forgets.”

There are many such examples. Thus, “intercession” is a verbal noun that necessarily involves both an intercessor and someone on whose behalf intercession is made. It encompasses the intercession of every intercessor and every intercession made on someone’s behalf. Hence, when He says:

“On that Day, intercession will be of no benefit,”

He negates both kinds: intercession by intercessors and intercession on behalf of sinners. His words,

“except one to whom the Most Merciful gives permission,”

encompass both categories: intercessors to whom the Most Merciful gives permission and whose words He approves, and those on whose behalf intercession is made, to whom the Most Merciful gives permission and whose words He approves.

Intercession benefits the one on whose behalf it is made by delivering him from punishment. It also benefits the intercessor: his intercession is accepted, he is honoured by its acceptance, and he is rewarded for it. On that Day, intercession will benefit neither an intercessor nor someone on whose behalf it is made,

“except one to whom the Most Merciful gives permission and who speaks rightly.”

This category—those who are granted permission and whose words are approved—comprises those who benefit from intercession. This accords with all the other verses. Sometimes He makes His permission a condition for intercession, as in His words:

“Who is there that can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

At other times, He makes bearing witness to the truth a condition, as in His words:

“Nor is power possessed by those who

---

## Page 7456

they invoke besides Him to intercede.” He then says:

“Except those who bear witness to the truth while they know.”

Here He stipulates both conditions: that the Most Merciful grant the person permission and that he speak rightly. The exception encompasses the action expressed by the verbal noun in relation to both its agent and its object. This is like saying, “Sowing is beneficial only at its proper time.” This encompasses both sowing by the cultivator and the sowing of the land. Here, however, He says:

“Except one to whom the Most Merciful gives permission.”

The exception has no expressly stated antecedent class, for no class from which this person is excepted has been mentioned beforehand. He merely says:

“Intercession benefits none except one to whom the Most Merciful gives permission.”

If nothing is understood to have been omitted, the meaning is that intercession benefits none except this category of people, for they do benefit from intercession. The meaning would thus be that it benefits both the intercessor and the one on whose behalf intercession is made.

If, however, an omission is understood, the implied wording being, “Intercession is of no benefit except the intercession of those to whom the Most Merciful gives permission,” then the verbal noun is attributed to both categories, each according to its respective role: to some because they intercede, and to others because intercession is made on their behalf. This would be like His words:

“But righteousness is one who believes in Allah”—that is, one who believes.

And:

“The likeness of those who disbelieve is that of one who calls out.”

That is, the likeness of one who calls the disbelievers is that of one who calls out; or the likeness of the disbelievers is that of one being called to—that is, one to whom the call is directed. In all these instances, the meaning is clear and understood. Hence, expressing it concisely rather than at length is among the most eloquent forms of speech.

---

## Page 7457

If His words,

“On that Day, intercession will be of no benefit,”

belong to this category, they cannot be adduced as proof that the intercessor benefits from intercession. Even if He does not honour him, the intercessor would be among those who benefit from intercession. In the other verse,

“Intercession is of no benefit with Him except for those to whom He gives permission,”

this includes people from both groups.

However, it may be said that the implied meaning is: intercession is of no benefit with Him except for someone on whose behalf He has permitted intercession, so that another is permitted to intercede for him. Permission would thus apply to both groups, while the benefit would be for the person receiving intercession, as in one of the two interpretations. Alternatively, the meaning is: it benefits only those to whom He gives permission, from either group. Just as permission applies to both groups, so too does the benefit. The intercessor benefits from intercession, and his benefit may be greater than that of the person for whom he intercedes. Hence the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said in an authentic hadith:

“Intercede, and you will be rewarded. Allah decrees through the words of His Prophet whatever He wills.”

Thus, among the greatest honours Allah bestows upon His servant Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is the intercession reserved exclusively for him. This is the Praiseworthy Station for which the first and the last generations will praise him.

On this interpretation, there is no need to understand anything as omitted from the verse. Rather, its meaning would be:

---

## Page 7458

on that Day, intercession will benefit neither an intercessor nor someone for whom intercession is made, “except one whom the Most Merciful permits and who speaks rightly.”

Thus, an authentic hadith reports that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“O descendants of ‘Abd Manāf, I can do nothing for you against Allah. O Safiyya, aunt of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, I can do nothing for you against Allah. O ‘Abbās, uncle of the Messenger of Allah, I can do nothing for you against Allah.”

Another authentic hadith states:

“Let me not find any of you coming on the Day of Resurrection carrying on his neck a bellowing camel, a bleating sheep, or fluttering sheets, saying, ‘Help me! Help me!’ I will say, ‘I conveyed the message to you. I can do nothing for you against Allah.’”

It is thus clear that His words,

“Those whom they invoke besides Him possess no power of intercession,”

and,

“They have no power to address Him,”

retain their full force. His words in the verse, “They have no power before Him,” are like the Prophet’s words, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, “I can do nothing for you against Allah.” They are also like Abraham’s words to his father:

“I can do nothing for you against Allah.”

This verse resembles the words of the Most High:

“Lord of the heavens and the earth and all that lies between them, the Most Merciful—they have no power to address Him.”

“On the Day when the Spirit and the angels stand in ranks

---

## Page 7459

, they will not speak except one whom the Most Merciful permits and who speaks rightly.” This is like His words:

“On that Day, intercession will not benefit except one whom the Most Merciful permits and whose words He approves.”

In both passages, He makes His permission a condition. There He mentions “speaking rightly,” and here He mentions “His approval of what one says.” Whoever speaks rightly, Allah approves of his words, for Allah approves only of what is right.

Two interpretations have been given of that verse:

The first is that it too refers to intercession, as Ibn al-Sā’ib said: “They possess no power of intercession except with His permission.”

The second is that created beings cannot speak to the Lord except with His permission. This was stated by Muqātil. Likewise, concerning His words, “They have no power to address Him,” Mujāhid said: “To speak.” This is part of the Quranic exegesis reliably transmitted from him. He was among the most knowledgeable of the Successors in Quranic exegesis—or the most knowledgeable of them.

Al-Thawrī said: “If an explanation of the Quran reaches you from Mujāhid, that is sufficient for you.”

Mujāhid said: “I went through the Quran with Ibn ‘Abbās, stopping him at every verse and asking him about it.” Al-Shāfi‘ī and Ahmad relied on him, as did al-Bukhārī in his Sahīh. This interpretation also encompasses “intercession.”

---

## Page 7460

In His words, “They have no power to address Him,” He mentions no exception, for no one has any independent right to address Allah. No created being possesses anything in which he shares ownership with the Creator. As we explained concerning His words, “Those whom they invoke besides Him possess no power of intercession,” this statement is general and unrestricted in scope. None of those invoked besides Him possesses the power of intercession under any circumstances. Rather, when Allah permits them, they intercede without possessing that power as their own. The same applies to His words, “They have no power to address Him.” This is the view of the early generations and the majority of Quranic commentators.

Some, however, said that this refers to the disbelievers: they have no power to address Allah on that Day.

Ibn ‘Atiyyah said: “In His words, ‘They have no power,’ the pronoun refers to the disbelievers. That is, they have no power—through His bounty and His acts of perfecting—to address Him with an excuse or anything else.”

This is an innovated interpretation and an outright error. The correct view is that of the majority and the early generations: this statement is general, as He says in another verse:

“Voices will be humbled before the Most Merciful, and you will hear nothing but a whisper.”

In the hadith concerning Allah’s manifestation, recorded in the Sahīh, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, when describing their passage over the Bridge:

“No one will speak except the messengers, and the messengers’ supplication will be: ‘O Allah, grant safety! Grant safety!’”

This will be during the passage over the Bridge, after the reckoning and the weighing of deeds. What, then, of the time before that?

---

## Page 7461

Intercession was sought from the greatest messengers, those of firm resolve, and each said:

“My Lord has become angry today as He has never been before and will never be again. I have done such and such. Myself! Myself! Myself!”

If even these do not venture to address Allah Most High in intercession, what, then, of others?

Moreover, this verse comes after the mention of the righteous and the people of Paradise, following the mention of the disbelievers. He says:

“Indeed, the righteous will have triumph: gardens and vineyards, and full-breasted maidens of equal age, and an overflowing cup. There they will hear neither idle talk nor falsehood—a reward from your Lord, an ample gift, from the Lord of the heavens and the earth and everything between them, the Most Merciful. They have no power to address Him.”

Then He says:

“On the Day when the Spirit and the angels stand in a row, none will speak except one whom the Most Merciful permits and who speaks what is right.”

He thus tells us that “the Spirit and the angels” will stand in a row without speaking. This explains precisely His words, “They have no power to address Him.” The Arabs say, “I have no power over so-and-so’s affairs,” or “I have no power over so-and-so,” meaning: I can do nothing concerning his affairs.

The utmost a person can do in relation to another is to address him, even if only to make a request. Yet in that setting they have no power in relation to Allah—not even to address Him. No one speaks except by His permission, and none speaks except one whom the Most Merciful permits and who speaks what is right. He, the Most High, says:

“Except for Abraham’s words to his father, ‘I will surely seek forgiveness for you, but I have no power to help you against

---

## Page 7462

Allah in any way.’” Thus Allah’s intimate friend stated that he had no power to help his father against Allah in any way. What, then, of anyone else?

Mujāhid also commented on the words:

“Except one whom the Most Merciful permits and who speaks what is right.”

He said: “He spoke the truth in this world and acted upon it.” This report and the preceding one were narrated by ‘Abd ibn Humayd. It was also reported that ‘Ikrimah explained “and who speaks what is right” by saying: “What is right is the declaration that there is no god but Allah.”

According to Mujāhid’s interpretation, the exception therefore refers to someone who has spoken good words and performed righteous deeds. As for His words in Surah Tā Hā:

“Intercession will be of no benefit except for one whom the Most Merciful permits and whose speech He approves.”

If you understand this verse in the same way as the other, then the intercession here is intercession in its general sense: intercession to secure good things and admission to Paradise. Thus, the two Sahīh collections relate:

“People will be distressed on the Day of Resurrection and will say, ‘Why do we not seek someone to intercede with our Lord so that He may relieve us of standing here?’”

This is a request for intercession so that judgment may be passed between them. The hadith of intercession also states:

“Admit through the right-hand gate those of your community who will not be called to account.”

This is intercession on behalf of the people of Paradise. Hence it has been said that

---

## Page 7463

these two kinds of intercession are exclusive to Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Others also intercede on behalf of sinners. Thus, His words:

“On that Day, intercession will be of no benefit except for one whom the Most Merciful permits and whose speech He approves.”

include intercession on behalf of those gathered for judgment generally, the people of Paradise, and those deserving punishment.

In this verse and the other, He, glorified be He, does not mention deeds. He says only, “and who speaks what is right,” and “whose speech He approves.” Yet the evidence establishes that someone who utters “right and approved words” is not praiseworthy unless those words are accompanied by righteous deeds. Nevertheless, the words themselves are approved, for Allah has said:

“To Him ascend good words.”

Al-Baghawī, Abū al-Faraj ibn al-Jawzī, and others have mentioned two interpretations of His words:

“Those whom they invoke besides Him have no power to intercede, except those who bear witness to the truth while knowing.”

The first is that the exception refers to the intercessor, with man (“who”) occupying the nominative position. The second is that it refers to the person on whose behalf intercession is made.

Abū al-Faraj said: There are two interpretations of this verse. The first is that “those whom they invoke besides Him” refers to their gods. He then excepted Jesus, ‘Uzayr, and the angels, saying, “except those who bear witness to the truth,” meaning the testimony that there is no god but Allah, “while knowing”—knowing in their hearts what they testify to with their tongues.

He said: This is the view of the majority, including Qatādah.

---

## Page 7464

The second interpretation is that “those whom they invoke” refers to Jesus, ‘Uzayr, and the angels whom the polytheists worshipped. These possess no power to intercede for anyone “except those who bear witness to the truth”—namely, the declaration of sincere devotion—“while knowing” that Allah created Jesus, ‘Uzayr, and the angels. This is the view of a group that includes Mujāhid.

Al-Baghawī said concerning the verse:

“Those whom they invoke besides Him have no power to intercede, except those who bear witness to the truth.”

These are Jesus, ‘Uzayr, and the angels, for they were worshipped besides Allah, and they have the power to intercede. According to this interpretation, man (“who”) occupies the nominative position. It has also been said that man occupies the genitive position, and that “those whom they invoke” refers to Jesus, ‘Uzayr, and the angels. This means that they possess no power to intercede except for those who bear witness to the truth. He said: The first interpretation is more correct.

I say: A number of scholars have reported the statements of Mujāhid and Qatādah, including Ibn Abī Hātim. Through his well-known chain of transmission—which meets the criteria of authenticity—he narrated that Mujāhid said concerning His words:

“Those whom they invoke besides Him have no power to intercede.”

These are Jesus, ‘Uzayr, and the angels. He means that Jesus, ‘Uzayr, and the angels do not intercede except for “those who bear witness to the truth,” knowing the truth. This is his wording. He treats shafa‘a (“to intercede”) as directly transitive, and likewise the wording of . . . (1)

On this interpretation, man would occupy the accusative position, not the genitive as al-Baghawī stated;

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) A blank in the original.

---

## Page 7465

for when the governing preposition is omitted, the noun takes the accusative. On this understanding, one may say shafaʿtuhu and shafaʿtu lahu (both meaning “I interceded for him”), just as one says nasahtuhu and nasahtu lahu (both meaning “I advised him”). Shafaʿa means “he became an intercessor for the petitioner.” That is, they neither intercede for nor assist any petitioner “except those who bear witness to the truth while knowing” that Allah is their Lord.

It was also narrated through his chain of transmission that Qatādah said concerning “except those who bear witness to the truth while knowing”: “The angels, Jesus, and ‘Uzayr.” That is, they were worshipped besides Allah, yet they have intercession and standing with Allah.

I say: Both interpretations are sound in meaning. However, the correct interpretation of the verse is that the exception is discontinuous. No one besides Allah possesses any power of intercession whatsoever. No one is excepted from this before Allah. For He did not say, “No one intercedes,” nor did He say, “Intercession is not made for anyone.” Rather, He said:

“Those whom they invoke besides Him have no power to intercede.”

Anyone invoked besides Allah has absolutely no power of intercession. Intercession by permission is not restricted to those worshipped besides Allah. The foremost of all intercessors—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—was not worshipped as the Messiah was. Nevertheless, he has an intercession that no one else has. It is therefore not fitting to affirm intercession for those invoked besides Allah to the exclusion of those who have not been invoked.

---

## Page 7466

Thus, whoever takes the exception to be continuous is in effect saying that those invoked besides Allah have no power to intercede unless they bear witness to the truth while knowing, or that they intercede only for those who bear witness to the truth while knowing. As for those who are not invoked besides Allah, their intercession for anyone remains unmentioned. This meaning neither befits the Quran nor accords with it. The occasion of the verse’s revelation also refutes it. Furthermore, His statement,

“Those whom they invoke besides Him have no power to intercede,”

includes everything worshipped besides Him, including idols. For their worshippers used to say, “They intercede for us.” Allah Most High says:

“They worship besides Allah what can neither harm nor benefit them, and say, ‘These are our intercessors with Allah.’ Say, ‘Do you inform Allah of something He does not know in the heavens or on earth?’”

If it is said that He excepted the angels and the prophets, this would encourage those who believe that the beings they worship besides Allah intercede for them. This further demonstrates the unsoundness of the aforementioned view narrated from Qatādah. For if the meaning were that those worshipped do not intercede unless they are angels or prophets, this would affirm that those worshipped intercede for their worshippers if they were

---

## Page 7467

righteous. The entire Quran refutes this meaning. Thus Allah Most High says:

“How many angels there are in the heavens whose intercession avails nothing, except after Allah gives permission for whom He wills and is pleased with.”

Allah Most High also says:

“They say, ‘The Most Merciful has taken a son.’ Glory be to Him! Rather, they are honoured servants. They do not precede Him in speech, and they act at His command. He knows what is before them and what is behind them. They intercede only for those with whom He is pleased, and they stand in awe of Him.”

He thus makes clear that they intercede only for those with whom the Lord is pleased. It follows that they must be granted permission concerning those for whom they intercede; they are not given unrestricted permission.

Moreover, whenever the Quran denies intercession independently of Him, it does so without qualification. His words “besides Him” may modify “have power,” “invoke,” or both. The meaning would therefore be: “Those whom they invoke have no power of intercession independently of Him”; or: “Those whom they invoke besides Him have no power to intercede.” The latter is more apparent, because He says:

“Those whom they invoke besides Him have no power to intercede.”

He places “intercession” last and “besides Him” before it. Such expressions occur frequently in the Quran: “they invoke besides Allah” and “they worship besides Allah,” as in His words:

“They worship besides Allah what can neither harm nor benefit them,”

and:

“Do not invoke besides Allah what can neither benefit nor harm you.”

This differs from saying: “Those whom they invoke have no power of intercession independently of Him.”

---

## Page 7468

For this wording has no parallel in the Quran. The wording used in such a case would be: “Those whom they invoke possess no power of intercession except by His permission,” or “for those with whom He is pleased,” or something similar. The expression “independently of Him” is not used in this sense, for intercession comes from Him. How, then, could it be independent of Him? It may, however, be with His permission or without His permission.

Moreover, if “those whom they invoke” is stated without qualification, it includes the Lord Most High, for they used to invoke Allah and invoke others alongside Him. Hence His words:

“And those who do not invoke another god alongside Allah.”

The third possible construction is: “Those whom they invoke besides Him possess no power of intercession independently of Him.” This is better than the preceding reading, but it is subject to the same objection as the first.

Another point that weakens both readings is that “intercession” is not followed by any qualifying phrase. Rather, He says:

“Those whom they invoke besides Him possess no power of intercession.”

He thus denies without qualification that they possess the power of intercession. This is the correct understanding: none of those invoked besides Allah possesses the power of intercession. For the owner of a thing is the one who disposes of it by his own will and power, whereas no one intercedes with the Lord Most High except by His permission. Thus, no created being possesses the power of intercession under any circumstances. The qualification “except by His permission” does not apply to this possession; it applies only to the act itself. Hence the words:

“Who is there that can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

---

## Page 7469

As for possession, however, no one other than Him can possess the power of intercession. No created being possesses it under any circumstances, nor is it conceivable that a prophet, let alone anyone below him, could possess it. Rather, this is impossible, just as it is impossible for him to be a creator and lord. This accords with His words:

“Say: Call upon those whom you claim besides Allah. They do not possess an atom’s weight in the heavens or on earth, nor do they have any share in either, nor does He have any helper among them.”

He thus denies their possession without qualification. Then He says:

“Intercession is of no benefit with Him except for those to whom He gives permission.”

He thus denies the benefit of intercession except for those whom He has excepted. He does not affirm that any created being possesses the power of intercession. Rather, to Him—glorified is He—belongs all dominion and all praise. He has no partner in His dominion. The Most High says:

“Blessed is He who sent down the Criterion upon His servant, so that he might be a warner to all the worlds.”

“He to whom belongs the dominion of the heavens and the earth, who has never taken a son and has no partner in His dominion, who created everything and ordained it in due measure.”

This is why, when He denies the existence of intercessors besides Him, He does so absolutely, without exception. An exception is made only when He does not qualify them as being besides Him. The Most High says:

“And warn with it those who fear being gathered before their Lord: they have no protector or intercessor besides Him.”

The Most High also says:

“And remind with it, lest a soul be doomed for what it has earned: it will have no protector or intercessor besides Allah.”

And the Most High says:

“You have no protector or intercessor besides Him.”

Thus, when He says “besides Him,” He denies intercession without qualification. When He mentions “by His permission,” He does not say “besides Him,” as in His words: “Who

---

## Page 7470

is there who can intercede with Him except by His permission?” And His words:

“There is no intercessor except after His permission.”

Whoever reflects upon the Quran will see that it is just as the Most High says:

“Allah has sent down the best discourse: a Book whose parts resemble one another and whose themes are reiterated.”

Its parts resemble and confirm one another; it is neither inconsistent nor contradictory:

“Had it been from anyone other than Allah, they would have found in it much inconsistency.”

It is “reiterated”: Allah reiterates its categories and treats them comprehensively. Realities are either mutually alike—corresponding to “resembling one another”—or similar, comprising classes, categories and types—corresponding to “reiterated.”

“Doubling” can denote repetition in general, without being restricted to just twice. Thus, in the Most High’s words, “Look again, twice,” repetition in general is intended. This is like saying, “I told him time after time,” meaning repeated occasions generally. Likewise, you may say, “He says such-and-such, and says such-and-such,” even though he has said it many times.

An example is the report of Hudhayfah ibn al-Yamān—may Allah be pleased with them both—concerning the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“He kept saying between the two prostrations, ‘My Lord, forgive me. My Lord, forgive me.’”

He did not mean that the Prophet said this only twice, as some people mistakenly suppose. Rather, he meant that he kept reiterating these words, repeating them again and again, just as he would repeat the words glorifying Allah.

---

## Page 7471

Hudhayfah—may Allah be pleased with him—also reported in the authentic hadith narrated by Muslim:

“He bowed for about as long as he had stood, saying during his bowing, ‘Glory be to my Lord, the Magnificent. Glory be to my Lord, the Magnificent.’ He also mentioned that he prostrated for about as long as he had stood, saying during his prostration, ‘My Lord, forgive me. My Lord, forgive me.’”

He explicitly stated in the authentic hadith:

“He prolonged his bowing and prostration for as long as it took to recite al-Baqarah, al-Nisā’ and Āl ‘Imrān.”

For he had recited all these surahs while standing. He also mentioned:

“He would say, ‘Glory be to my Lord, the Magnificent. Glory be to my Lord, the Magnificent. Glory be to my Lord, the Most High. Glory be to my Lord, the Most High.’”

It is thus clear that, by repeating the wording twice, he intended repetition in general, not restriction to two occurrences. For “two” is the first number indicating multiplicity. By mentioning this first number, he meant that the Prophet repeated these words rather than saying them only once. Thus, “doubling” denotes repetition, and repetition may involve different categories.

There is no mere repetition in the Quran; rather, every passage necessarily conveys benefits. Thus, “resembling one another” applies to corresponding passages that are alike, while “reiterated” applies to different types. Reiteration may also occur in passages that resemble one another: that is, a particular meaning is repeated in the Quran to convey further benefits.

---

## Page 7472

Thus, “reiterated” encompasses both senses. The Opening of the Book is “the seven oft-repeated verses” because it comprises both. A fuller discussion of this belongs elsewhere.

The point here is that His statement, “And those they invoke besides Him possess no power of intercession,” forms a complete statement in itself. None of those worshipped besides Allah possesses any power of intercession whatsoever. Then He makes an exception: “except those who bear witness to the truth knowingly.” This is a disconnected exception. Such an exception concerns a meaning shared by those mentioned. Thus, when He denied that they possess intercession, intercession remained without anyone possessing it.

It is as though the question had been asked: “If they do not possess it, do they intercede for anyone?” The answer is: “Yes: ‘those who bear witness to the truth knowingly.’” This includes both the intercessor and the one for whom intercession is made. None intercedes except those who bear witness to the truth knowingly. Although the angels, prophets and righteous do not possess intercession, they intercede when the Lord permits them. They are permitted to intercede only for the believers who testify that there is no god but Allah, thus bearing witness to the truth knowingly. They do not intercede for those who uttered these words merely in imitation of their forefathers and religious teachers.

As stated in the authentic hadith:

“A man is asked in his grave, ‘What do you say about this man?’ The believer says, ‘He is Allah’s servant and Messenger. He brought us clear proofs and guidance.’ But the one in doubt says, ‘Ah, ah! I do not know. I heard

---

## Page 7473

people saying something, so I said it.’” This is why He said:

“Except those who bear witness to the truth knowingly.”

Ibn ‘Abbās’s statement has already been cited: “This means those who say, ‘There is no god but Allah’”—meaning, sincerely from their hearts.

All the authentic hadiths concerning intercession make clear that intercession is only for those who profess, “There is no god but Allah.” It is established in Sahīh al-Bukhārī that Abū Hurayrah asked the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Who will be most fortunate in receiving your intercession on the Day of Resurrection?”

He replied: “Abū Hurayrah, I expected that no one would ask me about this hadith before you, given the eagerness for hadith I have seen in you. The most fortunate in receiving my intercession on the Day of Resurrection will be those who say, ‘There is no god but Allah,’ with sincerity from within themselves.”

He thus made clear that those who profess it with inward sincerity are more fortunate in receiving his intercession, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, than others who say it with their tongues while their words and deeds belie it.

These, then, are those who bear witness to the truth: they bear witness that “there is no god but Allah,” just as Allah bears witness to this concerning Himself, as do His angels and those endowed with knowledge:

“Allah bears witness that there is no god except

---

## Page 7474

Him, as do the angels and those endowed with knowledge; He upholds justice. There is no god except Him, the Almighty, the All-Wise.”

When they bear witness knowingly, they are among those entitled to intercession, both as intercessors and as those for whom intercession is made. For believers who affirm Allah’s oneness intercede for one another, as established in authentic hadiths.

Thus, in the lengthy hadith concerning divine manifestation and intercession, recorded in both Sahīh collections, Abū Saʿīd al-Khudrī, may Allah be pleased with him, reports that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Once the believers have been delivered from the Fire—by Him in Whose hand is my soul, none of you pleads more earnestly with Allah to secure his rights than the believers will plead with Allah on the Day of Resurrection for their brothers who are in the Fire. They will say, ‘Our Lord, they used to fast with us, pray, and perform the pilgrimage.’ They will be told, ‘Bring out those whom you recognize.’ Their forms will then be protected from the Fire.”

He then related the remainder of the hadith.

The reported occasion of the verse’s revelation also supports what he stated. Abū al-Faraj Ibn al-Jawzī said:

“The occasion of its revelation was that al-Nadr ibn al-Hārith and a group with him said, ‘If what Muhammad says is true, then we shall take the angels as our patrons, for they are more entitled to intercede than Muhammad.’ Then this verse was revealed. This was reported by Muqātil.”

---

## Page 7475

Accordingly, the point is that the angels and others do not possess the power of intercession. Your taking them as patrons and seeking their intercession does not oblige them to intercede for you. None of those invoked besides Allah possesses the power of intercession. But as for “those who bear witness to the truth knowingly,” Allah accepts intercession on their behalf.

Intercession is therefore attained through bearing witness to the truth: the testimony that there is no god except Allah. It is not attained by taking anyone besides Allah as a patron—neither angels, nor prophets, nor righteous people.

Whoever takes any of these as a patron, invokes him, makes a pilgrimage to his grave or a place associated with him, makes vows to him, swears by him, and presents offerings to him so that he will intercede for him, will gain no protection whatsoever from Allah through these acts. Rather, he will be among those furthest removed from receiving his intercession or that of anyone else. For intercession is only for those who worship Allah alone and devote their hearts and their religion sincerely to Him. Whoever takes anyone besides Allah as a patron associates partners with Him.

Thus, the words and acts of worship through which the polytheists seek intercession actually deprive them of it. Those who worshipped angels, prophets, the friends of Allah, and righteous people so that they would intercede for them were deprived of their intercession by the very worship and association of partners with their Lord through which they sought it. They were punished with the opposite of what they intended, because they associated with Allah that for which He had sent down no authority.

Many of those who have gone astray suppose that intercession

---

## Page 7476

is attained through these practices, which involve associating partners with Allah or consist entirely of such association, as the polytheists of old supposed. Christians likewise suppose this, as do those who have gone astray among people who profess Islam: they invoke someone other than Allah, make pilgrimages to his grave or a place associated with him, make vows to him, and swear by him. They imagine that, through these acts, he becomes an intercessor for them. Allah Most High says:

“Say: Call upon those whom you claim besides Him. They have no power to remove harm from you or to transfer it elsewhere.”

“Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of drawing near to their Lord, vying to be nearest to Him. They hope for His mercy and fear His punishment. Indeed, your Lord’s punishment is ever to be feared.”

A group of the early generations said: There were people who worshipped the Messiah, Ezra, and the angels. Allah explained that these had no power to remove harm from them or to transfer it elsewhere, just as He explained that they did not possess the power of intercession. There is no exception to this, even though Allah answers their supplications. Then He said:

“Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of drawing near to their Lord, vying to be nearest to Him. They hope for His mercy and fear His punishment. Indeed, your Lord’s punishment is ever to be feared.”

Thus, He explained that these supposed deities whom they invoke besides Allah hoped for Allah’s mercy, feared His punishment, and sought to draw near to Him through righteous deeds, like all His other believing servants. Allah Most High also says:

“Nor would he command you to take the angels and prophets as lords. Would he command you to disbelieve after you have submitted to Allah?”

People hold various misguided beliefs concerning intercession, which have been discussed at length elsewhere.

---

## Page 7477

Thus, many of them imagine that intercession results from a connection between the soul of the intercessor and the soul of the person on whose behalf he intercedes, as mentioned by Abū Hāmid al-Ghazālī and others. They say that whoever sends more blessings upon the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, has a greater claim to intercession than others. Likewise, whoever thinks more highly of someone and venerates him more has a greater claim to his intercession. This is mistaken. Rather, this is the very assertion of the polytheists who said: “We take the angels as our allies so that they may intercede for us.” They imagine that loving any of the angels, prophets, or righteous people and pledging allegiance to him is a means of securing his intercession. But this is not the case.

Rather, the basis of intercession is affirming Allah’s oneness and devoting one’s religion and every form of worship exclusively to Him. The more sincere a person’s devotion, the greater his claim to intercession, just as he has a greater claim to every other form of mercy.

Intercession originates with Allah, and its fulfilment rests with Him. No one intercedes except with His permission. He is the One who grants the intercessor permission, and He is the One who accepts his intercession on behalf of the person concerned. Intercession is merely one of the means through which Allah shows mercy to those of His servants upon whom He bestows His mercy.

Those with the greatest claim to His mercy are those who affirm His oneness and devote themselves sincerely to Him. Thus, the more completely a person puts into effect the sincere affirmation “There is no god but Allah”—in knowledge, belief, action, disavowal, allegiance, and enmity—the greater his claim to mercy.

---

## Page 7478

As for sinners whose evil deeds outweigh their good deeds, so that their scales are light and they deserve the Fire: those among them who affirm “There is no god but Allah” will be afflicted by the Fire because of their sins. Allah will cause them to die in the Fire, and it will burn them except for the parts used in prostration. Then Allah will bring them out of the Fire through intercession and admit them to Paradise, as the authentic hadiths relate.

This makes clear that the entire matter hinges on truly putting into effect the declaration of sincere devotion, “There is no god but Allah”—not, as the people of ignorance supposed, on associating partners with Allah by clinging to the dead and worshipping them. This has been discussed at length elsewhere.

The point here is this:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, would combine praise, which is the essence of gratitude, with affirming Allah’s oneness and seeking forgiveness when he raised his head from bowing. He would say: ‘Our Lord, to You belongs all praise, filling the heavens, filling the earth, filling all that lies between them, and filling whatever else You will. O You who are worthy of praise and glory! The truest words a servant can say—and we are all Your servants—are: None can withhold what You give, and none can give what You withhold, nor can anyone’s worldly fortune avail him against You.’ Then he would say: ‘O Allah, purify me with snow, hail, and cold water. O Allah, purify me of sins and misdeeds as a white garment is cleansed of dirt.’”

This was related by Muslim in his Sahīh from Abū Sa‘īd al-Khudrī, may Allah be pleased with him, who said: “Whenever the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, raised his head

---

## Page 7479
